Grade 6 / Revised 140-day curriculum

Day 001 / Unit 1: Add Decimals with Place Value

6.NS.B.3

I can add decimals by aligning equal place values.

enVision Topic 1: Use Positive Rational Numbers | Spectrum companion Chapter 1

Line up decimal points so tenths are under tenths and hundredths under hundredths. Add zeros if helpful. Add from right to left, regroup, and bring the decimal point straight down.

Worked example

Use the standard algorithm: 85.9 + 2.9. Show your regrouping or division steps.

Align decimal points; 85.9 + 2.9 = 88.8.

88.8

Worked example

Use the standard algorithm: 4.06 + 0.15. Show your regrouping or division steps. Select one answer, then justify it with a model or calculation. A. 521/100 B. 421/100 C. 321/100 D. 421/10

Align decimal points; 4.06 + 0.15 = 4.21.

B - 421/100

  1. Use the standard algorithm: 40.5 + 8.4. Show your regrouping or division steps.

  2. Use the standard algorithm: 84.7 + 2. Show your regrouping or division steps.

  3. Use the standard algorithm: 59.4 + 1.9. Show your regrouping or division steps.

  4. Use the standard algorithm: 1.67 + 2.5. Show your regrouping or division steps.

  5. Use the standard algorithm: 6.34 + 7.9. Show your regrouping or division steps.

  6. Use the standard algorithm: 4.93 + 6.7. Show your regrouping or division steps.

  7. Use the standard algorithm: 5.68 + 0.8. Show your regrouping or division steps.

  8. Use the standard algorithm: 7.99 + 0.47. Show your regrouping or division steps. Select one answer, then justify it with a model or calculation. A. 423/5 B. 423/50 C. 473/50 D. 373/50

  9. Use the standard algorithm: 2.67 + 0.21. Show your regrouping or division steps. Select one answer, then justify it with a model or calculation. A. 72/25 B. 144/5 C. 97/25 D. 47/25

  10. A runner walks 4.4 km in the morning and 0.71 km later. What is the total distance?

Answer key
  1. 48.9

    Align decimal points; 40.5 + 8.4 = 48.9.

  2. 86.7

    Align decimal points; 84.7 + 2 = 86.7.

  3. 61.3

    Align decimal points; 59.4 + 1.9 = 61.3.

  4. 4.17

    Align decimal points; 1.67 + 2.5 = 4.17.

  5. 14.24

    Align decimal points; 6.34 + 7.9 = 14.24.

  6. 11.63

    Align decimal points; 4.93 + 6.7 = 11.63.

  7. 6.48

    Align decimal points; 5.68 + 0.8 = 6.48.

  8. B - 423/50

    Align decimal points; 7.99 + 0.47 = 8.46.

  9. A - 72/25

    Align decimal points; 2.67 + 0.21 = 2.88.

  10. 5.11 km

    Align decimal points; 4.4 + 0.71 = 5.11.

Day 002 / Unit 1: Subtract Decimals and Check

6.NS.B.3

I can subtract decimals and explain regrouping.

enVision Topic 1: Use Positive Rational Numbers | Spectrum companion Chapter 1

Align decimal points and add placeholder zeros. Regroup one whole as ten tenths, or one tenth as ten hundredths. Subtract by place and check by adding the difference to the subtrahend.

Worked example

Use the standard algorithm: 94.7 - 4.1. Show your regrouping or division steps.

Align decimal points; 94.7 - 4.1 = 90.6.

90.6

Worked example

Use the standard algorithm: 5.37 - 0.49. Show your regrouping or division steps. Select one answer, then justify it with a model or calculation. A. 122/25 B. 147/25 C. 97/25 D. 244/5

Align decimal points; 5.37 - 0.49 = 4.88.

A - 122/25

  1. Use the standard algorithm: 46.2 - 7.3. Show your regrouping or division steps.

  2. Use the standard algorithm: 82.5 - 1.5. Show your regrouping or division steps.

  3. Use the standard algorithm: 36.6 - 7.2. Show your regrouping or division steps.

  4. Use the standard algorithm: 8.2 - 4.5. Show your regrouping or division steps.

  5. Use the standard algorithm: 8.04 - 6. Show your regrouping or division steps.

  6. Use the standard algorithm: 6.47 - 1.4. Show your regrouping or division steps.

  7. Use the standard algorithm: 5.45 - 0.35. Show your regrouping or division steps.

  8. Use the standard algorithm: 8.54 - 0.34. Show your regrouping or division steps. Select one answer, then justify it with a model or calculation. A. 46/5 B. 36/5 C. 82 D. 41/5

  9. Use the standard algorithm: 6.71 - 0.14. Show your regrouping or division steps. Select one answer, then justify it with a model or calculation. A. 757/100 B. 657/100 C. 657/10 D. 557/100

  10. A tank holds 5.59 L. A class uses 0.69 L. How much remains?

Answer key
  1. 38.9

    Align decimal points; 46.2 - 7.3 = 38.9.

  2. 81

    Align decimal points; 82.5 - 1.5 = 81.

  3. 29.4

    Align decimal points; 36.6 - 7.2 = 29.4.

  4. 3.7

    Align decimal points; 8.2 - 4.5 = 3.7.

  5. 2.04

    Align decimal points; 8.04 - 6 = 2.04.

  6. 5.07

    Align decimal points; 6.47 - 1.4 = 5.07.

  7. 5.1

    Align decimal points; 5.45 - 0.35 = 5.1.

  8. D - 41/5

    Align decimal points; 8.54 - 0.34 = 8.2.

  9. B - 657/100

    Align decimal points; 6.71 - 0.14 = 6.57.

  10. 4.9 L

    Align decimal points; 5.59 - 0.69 = 4.9.

Day 003 / Unit 1: Multiply Decimals and Estimate

6.NS.B.3

I can multiply decimals and use an estimate to place the decimal point.

enVision Topic 1: Use Positive Rational Numbers | Spectrum companion Chapter 1

Multiply as whole numbers using the standard algorithm. The product has the total number of decimal places in the two factors before trailing zeros are removed. Check its size against an estimate.

Worked example

Use the standard algorithm: 36.8 × 7.3. Show your regrouping or division steps.

Compute the whole-number product and restore decimal places: 36.8 × 7.3 = 268.64.

268.64

Worked example

Use the standard algorithm: 4.06 × 0.27. Show your regrouping or division steps. Select one answer, then justify it with a model or calculation. A. 481/5000 B. 5481/5000 C. 5481/500 D. 10481/5000

Compute the whole-number product and restore decimal places: 4.06 × 0.27 = 1.0962.

B - 5481/5000

  1. Use the standard algorithm: 19.2 × 1.8. Show your regrouping or division steps.

  2. Use the standard algorithm: 46.8 × 2.3. Show your regrouping or division steps.

  3. Use the standard algorithm: 22.3 × 3.7. Show your regrouping or division steps.

  4. Use the standard algorithm: 7.17 × 7.4. Show your regrouping or division steps.

  5. Use the standard algorithm: 9.39 × 5.1. Show your regrouping or division steps.

  6. Use the standard algorithm: 3.82 × 3.1. Show your regrouping or division steps.

  7. Use the standard algorithm: 3 × 0.62. Show your regrouping or division steps.

  8. Use the standard algorithm: 6.92 × 0.8. Show your regrouping or division steps. Select one answer, then justify it with a model or calculation. A. 817/125 B. 692/125 C. 567/125 D. 1384/25

  9. Use the standard algorithm: 7.12 × 0.52. Show your regrouping or division steps. Select one answer, then justify it with a model or calculation. A. 2939/625 B. 1689/625 C. 2314/625 D. 4628/125

  10. Ribbon costs $7.07 per meter. What is the exact cost of 0.44 meters? Do not round.

Answer key
  1. 34.56

    Compute the whole-number product and restore decimal places: 19.2 × 1.8 = 34.56.

  2. 107.64

    Compute the whole-number product and restore decimal places: 46.8 × 2.3 = 107.64.

  3. 82.51

    Compute the whole-number product and restore decimal places: 22.3 × 3.7 = 82.51.

  4. 53.058

    Compute the whole-number product and restore decimal places: 7.17 × 7.4 = 53.058.

  5. 47.889

    Compute the whole-number product and restore decimal places: 9.39 × 5.1 = 47.889.

  6. 11.842

    Compute the whole-number product and restore decimal places: 3.82 × 3.1 = 11.842.

  7. 1.86

    Compute the whole-number product and restore decimal places: 3 × 0.62 = 1.86.

  8. B - 692/125

    Compute the whole-number product and restore decimal places: 6.92 × 0.8 = 5.536.

  9. C - 2314/625

    Compute the whole-number product and restore decimal places: 7.12 × 0.52 = 3.7024.

  10. 3.1108 dollars

    Compute the whole-number product and restore decimal places: 7.07 × 0.44 = 3.1108.

Day 004 / Unit 1: Divide Whole Numbers

6.NS.B.2

I can divide multi-digit whole numbers and check the quotient.

enVision Topic 1: Use Positive Rational Numbers | Spectrum companion Chapter 1

Estimate the quotient first. Divide, multiply, subtract, and bring down one digit at a time. Keep each quotient digit in its place; write zero when needed. Check dividend = divisor × quotient + remainder.

Worked example

Use long division: 304 ÷ 4. Show the algorithm and check.

30 ÷ 4: write 7, subtract 28, remainder 2; bring down the next digit: 24 ÷ 4: write 6, subtract 24, remainder 0. Check: 4 × 76 = 304.

76

Worked example

Use long division: 2,948 ÷ 23. Show the algorithm and check.

29 ÷ 23: write 1, subtract 23, remainder 6; bring down the next digit: 64 ÷ 23: write 2, subtract 46, remainder 18; bring down the next digit: 188 ÷ 23: write 8, subtract 184, remainder 4. Check: 23 × 128 + 4 = 2948.

128 R 4

  1. Use long division: 536 ÷ 8. Show the algorithm and check.

  2. Use long division: 256 ÷ 4. Show the algorithm and check.

  3. Use long division: 592 ÷ 8. Show the algorithm and check.

  4. Use long division: 6,194 ÷ 19. Show the algorithm and check.

  5. Use long division: 7,854 ÷ 22. Show the algorithm and check.

  6. Use long division: 4,008 ÷ 12. Show the algorithm and check.

  7. Use long division: 4,255 ÷ 17. Show the algorithm and check.

  8. Use long division: 15,080 ÷ 37. Show the algorithm and check.

  9. Use long division: 2,900 ÷ 19. Show the algorithm and check.

  10. A club has 5132 cards. Each full packet holds 13. How many full packets can it make, and how many cards remain?

Answer key
  1. 67

    53 ÷ 8: write 6, subtract 48, remainder 5; bring down the next digit: 56 ÷ 8: write 7, subtract 56, remainder 0. Check: 8 × 67 = 536.

  2. 64

    25 ÷ 4: write 6, subtract 24, remainder 1; bring down the next digit: 16 ÷ 4: write 4, subtract 16, remainder 0. Check: 4 × 64 = 256.

  3. 74

    59 ÷ 8: write 7, subtract 56, remainder 3; bring down the next digit: 32 ÷ 8: write 4, subtract 32, remainder 0. Check: 8 × 74 = 592.

  4. 326

    61 ÷ 19: write 3, subtract 57, remainder 4; bring down the next digit: 49 ÷ 19: write 2, subtract 38, remainder 11; bring down the next digit: 114 ÷ 19: write 6, subtract 114, remainder 0. Check: 19 × 326 = 6194.

  5. 357

    78 ÷ 22: write 3, subtract 66, remainder 12; bring down the next digit: 125 ÷ 22: write 5, subtract 110, remainder 15; bring down the next digit: 154 ÷ 22: write 7, subtract 154, remainder 0. Check: 22 × 357 = 7854.

  6. 334

    40 ÷ 12: write 3, subtract 36, remainder 4; bring down the next digit: 40 ÷ 12: write 3, subtract 36, remainder 4; bring down the next digit: 48 ÷ 12: write 4, subtract 48, remainder 0. Check: 12 × 334 = 4008.

  7. 250 R 5

    42 ÷ 17: write 2, subtract 34, remainder 8; bring down the next digit: 85 ÷ 17: write 5, subtract 85, remainder 0; bring down the next digit: 5 ÷ 17: write 0, subtract 0, remainder 5. Check: 17 × 250 + 5 = 4255.

  8. 407 R 21

    150 ÷ 37: write 4, subtract 148, remainder 2; bring down the next digit: 28 ÷ 37: write 0, subtract 0, remainder 28; bring down the next digit: 280 ÷ 37: write 7, subtract 259, remainder 21. Check: 37 × 407 + 21 = 15080.

  9. 152 R 12

    29 ÷ 19: write 1, subtract 19, remainder 10; bring down the next digit: 100 ÷ 19: write 5, subtract 95, remainder 5; bring down the next digit: 50 ÷ 19: write 2, subtract 38, remainder 12. Check: 19 × 152 + 12 = 2900.

  10. 394 full packets; 10 cards remain

    5132 = 13 × 394 + 10.

Day 005 / Unit 1: Estimate and Check Whole-Number Division

6.NS.B.2

I can divide multi-digit whole numbers and check the quotient.

enVision Topic 1: Use Positive Rational Numbers | Spectrum companion Chapter 1

Estimate the quotient first. Divide, multiply, subtract, and bring down one digit at a time. Keep each quotient digit in its place; write zero when needed. Check dividend = divisor × quotient + remainder.

Worked example

Use long division: 175 ÷ 5. Show the algorithm and check.

17 ÷ 5: write 3, subtract 15, remainder 2; bring down the next digit: 25 ÷ 5: write 5, subtract 25, remainder 0. Check: 5 × 35 = 175.

35

Worked example

Use long division: 5,554 ÷ 35. Show the algorithm and check.

55 ÷ 35: write 1, subtract 35, remainder 20; bring down the next digit: 205 ÷ 35: write 5, subtract 175, remainder 30; bring down the next digit: 304 ÷ 35: write 8, subtract 280, remainder 24. Check: 35 × 158 + 24 = 5554.

158 R 24

  1. Use long division: 729 ÷ 9. Show the algorithm and check.

  2. Use long division: 252 ÷ 4. Show the algorithm and check.

  3. Use long division: 430 ÷ 5. Show the algorithm and check.

  4. Use long division: 2,299 ÷ 19. Show the algorithm and check.

  5. Use long division: 2,716 ÷ 14. Show the algorithm and check.

  6. Use long division: 5,928 ÷ 38. Show the algorithm and check.

  7. Use long division: 6,701 ÷ 21. Show the algorithm and check.

  8. Use long division: 2,368 ÷ 15. Show the algorithm and check.

  9. Use long division: 11,312 ÷ 37. Show the algorithm and check.

  10. A club has 4822 cards. Each full packet holds 13. How many full packets can it make, and how many cards remain?

Answer key
  1. 81

    72 ÷ 9: write 8, subtract 72, remainder 0; bring down the next digit: 9 ÷ 9: write 1, subtract 9, remainder 0. Check: 9 × 81 = 729.

  2. 63

    25 ÷ 4: write 6, subtract 24, remainder 1; bring down the next digit: 12 ÷ 4: write 3, subtract 12, remainder 0. Check: 4 × 63 = 252.

  3. 86

    43 ÷ 5: write 8, subtract 40, remainder 3; bring down the next digit: 30 ÷ 5: write 6, subtract 30, remainder 0. Check: 5 × 86 = 430.

  4. 121

    22 ÷ 19: write 1, subtract 19, remainder 3; bring down the next digit: 39 ÷ 19: write 2, subtract 38, remainder 1; bring down the next digit: 19 ÷ 19: write 1, subtract 19, remainder 0. Check: 19 × 121 = 2299.

  5. 194

    27 ÷ 14: write 1, subtract 14, remainder 13; bring down the next digit: 131 ÷ 14: write 9, subtract 126, remainder 5; bring down the next digit: 56 ÷ 14: write 4, subtract 56, remainder 0. Check: 14 × 194 = 2716.

  6. 156

    59 ÷ 38: write 1, subtract 38, remainder 21; bring down the next digit: 212 ÷ 38: write 5, subtract 190, remainder 22; bring down the next digit: 228 ÷ 38: write 6, subtract 228, remainder 0. Check: 38 × 156 = 5928.

  7. 319 R 2

    67 ÷ 21: write 3, subtract 63, remainder 4; bring down the next digit: 40 ÷ 21: write 1, subtract 21, remainder 19; bring down the next digit: 191 ÷ 21: write 9, subtract 189, remainder 2. Check: 21 × 319 + 2 = 6701.

  8. 157 R 13

    23 ÷ 15: write 1, subtract 15, remainder 8; bring down the next digit: 86 ÷ 15: write 5, subtract 75, remainder 11; bring down the next digit: 118 ÷ 15: write 7, subtract 105, remainder 13. Check: 15 × 157 + 13 = 2368.

  9. 305 R 27

    113 ÷ 37: write 3, subtract 111, remainder 2; bring down the next digit: 21 ÷ 37: write 0, subtract 0, remainder 21; bring down the next digit: 212 ÷ 37: write 5, subtract 185, remainder 27. Check: 37 × 305 + 27 = 11312.

  10. 370 full packets; 12 cards remain

    4822 = 13 × 370 + 12.

I can divide multi-digit whole numbers and check the quotient.

enVision Topic 1: Use Positive Rational Numbers | Spectrum companion Chapter 1

Estimate the quotient first. Divide, multiply, subtract, and bring down one digit at a time. Keep each quotient digit in its place; write zero when needed. Check dividend = divisor × quotient + remainder.

Worked example

Use long division: 525 ÷ 5. Show the algorithm and check.

5 ÷ 5: write 1, subtract 5, remainder 0; bring down the next digit: 2 ÷ 5: write 0, subtract 0, remainder 2; bring down the next digit: 25 ÷ 5: write 5, subtract 25, remainder 0. Check: 5 × 105 = 525.

105

Day 007 / Unit 1: Divide by a Decimal

6.NS.B.3

I can divide by a decimal using an equivalent whole-number divisor.

enVision Topic 1: Use Positive Rational Numbers | Spectrum companion Chapter 1

Multiply both dividend and divisor by the same power of 10 so the divisor is a whole number. Then use long division, adding zeros to the dividend as needed. Check by multiplication.

Worked example

Use the standard algorithm: 46.8 ÷ 0.9. Show your regrouping or division steps.

Multiply both numbers by 10: 468 ÷ 9 = 52. Check: 0.9 × 52 = 46.8.

52

Worked example

Use the standard algorithm: 1.752 ÷ 0.24. Show your regrouping or division steps. Select one answer, then justify it with a model or calculation. A. 73 B. 83/10 C. 63/10 D. 73/10

Multiply both numbers by 100: 175.2 ÷ 24 = 7.3. Check: 0.24 × 7.3 = 1.752.

D - 73/10

  1. Use the standard algorithm: 55.3 ÷ 0.7. Show your regrouping or division steps.

  2. Use the standard algorithm: 15.4 ÷ 0.2. Show your regrouping or division steps.

  3. Use the standard algorithm: 21.5 ÷ 0.5. Show your regrouping or division steps.

  4. Use the standard algorithm: 6.45 ÷ 1.5. Show your regrouping or division steps.

  5. Use the standard algorithm: 20.4 ÷ 4. Show your regrouping or division steps.

  6. Use the standard algorithm: 8.64 ÷ 1.2. Show your regrouping or division steps.

  7. Use the standard algorithm: 2.16 ÷ 0.4. Show your regrouping or division steps.

  8. Use the standard algorithm: 0.624 ÷ 0.24. Show your regrouping or division steps. Select one answer, then justify it with a model or calculation. A. 8/5 B. 18/5 C. 13/5 D. 26

  9. Use the standard algorithm: 3.456 ÷ 0.64. Show your regrouping or division steps. Select one answer, then justify it with a model or calculation. A. 32/5 B. 22/5 C. 54 D. 27/5

  10. A container has 1.664 kg of seed. Each bag holds 0.32 kg. How many bags can be filled?

Answer key
  1. 79

    Multiply both numbers by 10: 553 ÷ 7 = 79. Check: 0.7 × 79 = 55.3.

  2. 77

    Multiply both numbers by 10: 154 ÷ 2 = 77. Check: 0.2 × 77 = 15.4.

  3. 43

    Multiply both numbers by 10: 215 ÷ 5 = 43. Check: 0.5 × 43 = 21.5.

  4. 4.3

    Multiply both numbers by 10: 64.5 ÷ 15 = 4.3. Check: 1.5 × 4.3 = 6.45.

  5. 5.1

    Multiply both numbers by 10: 204 ÷ 40 = 5.1. Check: 4 × 5.1 = 20.4.

  6. 7.2

    Multiply both numbers by 10: 86.4 ÷ 12 = 7.2. Check: 1.2 × 7.2 = 8.64.

  7. 5.4

    Multiply both numbers by 10: 21.6 ÷ 4 = 5.4. Check: 0.4 × 5.4 = 2.16.

  8. C - 13/5

    Multiply both numbers by 100: 62.4 ÷ 24 = 2.6. Check: 0.24 × 2.6 = 0.624.

  9. D - 27/5

    Multiply both numbers by 100: 345.6 ÷ 64 = 5.4. Check: 0.64 × 5.4 = 3.456.

  10. 5.2 bags

    Multiply both numbers by 100: 166.4 ÷ 32 = 5.2. Check: 0.32 × 5.2 = 1.664.

Day 008 / Unit 1: Explain Equivalent Decimal Division

6.NS.B.3

I can divide by a decimal using an equivalent whole-number divisor.

enVision Topic 1: Use Positive Rational Numbers | Spectrum companion Chapter 1

Multiply both dividend and divisor by the same power of 10 so the divisor is a whole number. Then use long division, adding zeros to the dividend as needed. Check by multiplication.

Worked example

Multiply both numbers by 10: 168 ÷ 8 = 21. Check: 0.8 × 21 = 16.8.

21

  7. Use the standard algorithm: 2.92 ÷ 0.4. Show your regrouping or division steps.

  9. Use the standard algorithm: 0.48 ÷ 0.4. Show your regrouping or division steps. Select one answer, then justify it with a model or calculation. A. 6/5 B. 1/5 C. 12 D. 11/5

Answer key
  1. 32

    Multiply both numbers by 10: 352 ÷ 8 = 44. Check: 0.8 × 44 = 35.2.

    Multiply both numbers by 10: 160 ÷ 32 = 5. Check: 3.2 × 5 = 16.

  7. 7.3

    Multiply both numbers by 10: 29.2 ÷ 4 = 7.3. Check: 0.4 × 7.3 = 2.92.

  8. A - 31/5

    Multiply both numbers by 100: 74.4 ÷ 12 = 6.2. Check: 0.12 × 6.2 = 0.744.

  9. A - 6/5

    Multiply both numbers by 10: 4.8 ÷ 4 = 1.2. Check: 0.4 × 1.2 = 0.48.

  10. 7.9 bags

    Multiply both numbers by 10: 31.6 ÷ 4 = 7.9. Check: 0.4 × 7.9 = 3.16.

Day 009 / Unit 1: Apply Decimal Operations

6.NS.B.3

I can apply Decimal Operations, and explain and check my reasoning.

enVision Topic 1: Use Positive Rational Numbers | Spectrum companion Chapter 1

Line up decimal points so tenths are under tenths and hundredths under hundredths. Add zeros if helpful. Add from right to left, regroup, and bring the decimal point straight down. Align decimal points and add placeholder zeros. Regroup one whole as ten tenths, or one tenth as ten hundredths. Subtract by place and check by adding the difference to the subtrahend. Multiply as whole numbers using the standard algorithm. The product has the total number of decimal places in the two factors before trailing zeros are removed. Check its size against an estimate. Multiply both dividend and divisor by the same power of 10 so the divisor is a whole number. Then use long division, adding zeros to the dividend as needed. Check by multiplication.

Worked example

Use the standard algorithm: 92.9 + 4.2. Show your regrouping or division steps.

Align decimal points; 92.9 + 4.2 = 97.1.

97.1

Worked example

Use the standard algorithm: 2.112 ÷ 0.64. Show your regrouping or division steps. Select one answer, then justify it with a model or calculation. A. 33/10 B. 43/10 C. 23/10 D. 33

Multiply both numbers by 100: 211.2 ÷ 64 = 3.3. Check: 0.64 × 3.3 = 2.112.

A - 33/10

  1. Use the standard algorithm: 47.3 + 6.8. Show your regrouping or division steps.

  2. Use the standard algorithm: 36.3 - 2.4. Show your regrouping or division steps.

  3. Use the standard algorithm: 90.3 × 6.5. Show your regrouping or division steps.

  4. Use the standard algorithm: 12 ÷ 2.5. Show your regrouping or division steps.

  5. Use the standard algorithm: 5.05 + 6.7. Show your regrouping or division steps.

  6. Use the standard algorithm: 5.77 - 4.1. Show your regrouping or division steps.

  7. Use the standard algorithm: 1.58 × 0.35. Show your regrouping or division steps.

  8. Use the standard algorithm: 0.3 ÷ 0.25. Show your regrouping or division steps. Select one answer, then justify it with a model or calculation. A. 11/5 B. 1/5 C. 12 D. 6/5

  9. Use the standard algorithm: 5.31 + 0.24. Show your regrouping or division steps. Select one answer, then justify it with a model or calculation. A. 111/2 B. 131/20 C. 111/20 D. 91/20

  10. A tank holds 1.26 L. A class uses 0.67 L. How much remains?

Answer key
  1. 54.1

    Align decimal points; 47.3 + 6.8 = 54.1.

  2. 33.9

    Align decimal points; 36.3 - 2.4 = 33.9.

  3. 586.95

    Compute the whole-number product and restore decimal places: 90.3 × 6.5 = 586.95.

  4. 4.8

    Multiply both numbers by 10: 120 ÷ 25 = 4.8. Check: 2.5 × 4.8 = 12.

  5. 11.75

    Align decimal points; 5.05 + 6.7 = 11.75.

  6. 1.67

    Align decimal points; 5.77 - 4.1 = 1.67.

  7. 0.553

    Compute the whole-number product and restore decimal places: 1.58 × 0.35 = 0.553.

  8. D - 6/5

    Multiply both numbers by 100: 30 ÷ 25 = 1.2. Check: 0.25 × 1.2 = 0.3.

  9. C - 111/20

    Align decimal points; 5.31 + 0.24 = 5.55.

  10. 0.59 L

    Align decimal points; 1.26 - 0.67 = 0.59.

Day 012 / Unit 1: Interpret Whole-Number Remainders

6.NS.B.2

I can divide multi-digit whole numbers and check the quotient.

enVision Topic 1: Use Positive Rational Numbers | Spectrum companion Chapter 1

Estimate the quotient first. Divide, multiply, subtract, and bring down one digit at a time. Keep each quotient digit in its place; write zero when needed. Check dividend = divisor × quotient + remainder.

Worked example

Use long division: 546 ÷ 7. Show the algorithm and check.

54 ÷ 7: write 7, subtract 49, remainder 5; bring down the next digit: 56 ÷ 7: write 8, subtract 56, remainder 0. Check: 7 × 78 = 546.

78

Worked example

Use long division: 11,312 ÷ 33. Show the algorithm and check.

113 ÷ 33: write 3, subtract 99, remainder 14; bring down the next digit: 141 ÷ 33: write 4, subtract 132, remainder 9; bring down the next digit: 92 ÷ 33: write 2, subtract 66, remainder 26. Check: 33 × 342 + 26 = 11312.

342 R 26

  1. Use long division: 450 ÷ 6. Show the algorithm and check.

  2. Use long division: 448 ÷ 8. Show the algorithm and check.

  3. Use long division: 440 ÷ 5. Show the algorithm and check.

  4. Use long division: 6,647 ÷ 23. Show the algorithm and check.

  5. Use long division: 6,325 ÷ 23. Show the algorithm and check.

  6. Use long division: 2,327 ÷ 13. Show the algorithm and check.

  7. Use long division: 11,533 ÷ 30. Show the algorithm and check.

  8. Use long division: 13,555 ÷ 36. Show the algorithm and check.

  9. Use long division: 4,262 ÷ 34. Show the algorithm and check.

  10. A club has 6605 cards. Each full packet holds 25. How many full packets can it make, and how many cards remain?

Answer key
  1. 75

    45 ÷ 6: write 7, subtract 42, remainder 3; bring down the next digit: 30 ÷ 6: write 5, subtract 30, remainder 0. Check: 6 × 75 = 450.

  2. 56

    44 ÷ 8: write 5, subtract 40, remainder 4; bring down the next digit: 48 ÷ 8: write 6, subtract 48, remainder 0. Check: 8 × 56 = 448.

  3. 88

    44 ÷ 5: write 8, subtract 40, remainder 4; bring down the next digit: 40 ÷ 5: write 8, subtract 40, remainder 0. Check: 5 × 88 = 440.

  4. 289

    66 ÷ 23: write 2, subtract 46, remainder 20; bring down the next digit: 204 ÷ 23: write 8, subtract 184, remainder 20; bring down the next digit: 207 ÷ 23: write 9, subtract 207, remainder 0. Check: 23 × 289 = 6647.

  5. 275

    63 ÷ 23: write 2, subtract 46, remainder 17; bring down the next digit: 172 ÷ 23: write 7, subtract 161, remainder 11; bring down the next digit: 115 ÷ 23: write 5, subtract 115, remainder 0. Check: 23 × 275 = 6325.

  6. 179

    23 ÷ 13: write 1, subtract 13, remainder 10; bring down the next digit: 102 ÷ 13: write 7, subtract 91, remainder 11; bring down the next digit: 117 ÷ 13: write 9, subtract 117, remainder 0. Check: 13 × 179 = 2327.

  7. 384 R 13

    115 ÷ 30: write 3, subtract 90, remainder 25; bring down the next digit: 253 ÷ 30: write 8, subtract 240, remainder 13; bring down the next digit: 133 ÷ 30: write 4, subtract 120, remainder 13. Check: 30 × 384 + 13 = 11533.

  8. 376 R 19

    135 ÷ 36: write 3, subtract 108, remainder 27; bring down the next digit: 275 ÷ 36: write 7, subtract 252, remainder 23; bring down the next digit: 235 ÷ 36: write 6, subtract 216, remainder 19. Check: 36 × 376 + 19 = 13555.

  9. 125 R 12

    42 ÷ 34: write 1, subtract 34, remainder 8; bring down the next digit: 86 ÷ 34: write 2, subtract 68, remainder 18; bring down the next digit: 182 ÷ 34: write 5, subtract 170, remainder 12. Check: 34 × 125 + 12 = 4262.

  10. 264 full packets; 5 cards remain

    6605 = 25 × 264 + 5.

Day 016 / Unit 2: Multiply Fractions: Readiness

6.NS.A.1

I can use fraction multiplication to prepare for fraction division.

enVision Topic 1: Use Positive Rational Numbers | Spectrum companion Chapter 2

For a product of fractions, multiply numerators and multiply denominators, then simplify. Convert mixed numbers to improper fractions first. Multiplying by a fraction less than 1 makes a positive quantity smaller.

Worked example

Calculate 1/4 × 1/2. Simplify.

1/4 × 1/2 = 1/8.

1/8

Worked example

Calculate 3 × 5/8. Simplify and explain why your answer is reasonable. Select one answer, then justify it with a model or calculation. A. 23/8 B. 8/15 C. 7/8 D. 15/8

3 × 5/8 = 15/8.

D - 15/8

Day 018 / Unit 2: Model Fraction Division with Bars

enVision Topic 1: Use Positive Rational Numbers | Spectrum companion Chapter 2

A quotient asks how many groups of the divisor fit in the dividend. Rename both fractions with a common denominator, draw equal parts, and count groups. A partial group is a fraction of the divisor, not of the original whole.

Worked example

Each small part is 1/6; 3 shaded parts divided into groups of 3 gives 3/3 = 1 groups.

Day 020 / Unit 2: Connect Fraction Bars and Related Equations

6.NS.A.1

I can connect Fraction Bars and Related Equations, and explain and check my reasoning.

enVision Topic 1: Use Positive Rational Numbers | Spectrum companion Chapter 2

A quotient asks how many groups of the divisor fit in the dividend. Rename both fractions with a common denominator, draw equal parts, and count groups. A partial group is a fraction of the divisor, not of the original whole. Division and multiplication are inverse operations. For T ÷ s = n, the related equation is s × n = T. Label whether the unknown is the total, the group size, or the number of groups.

Worked example

Draw a bar partitioned into 6 equal parts. Shade 5 parts. Circle groups of 4 parts. Use the model to find 5/6 ÷ 2/3.

Each small part is 1/6; 5 shaded parts divided into groups of 4 gives 5/4 = 5/4 groups.

5/4

Worked example

The equation 1/5 × n = 1 represents equally sized portions. Find n, write the related division equation, and check.

1 ÷ 1/5 = 1 × 5 = 5. Check: 1/5 × 5 = 1.

n = 5; 1 ÷ 1/5 = 5

  1. Draw a bar partitioned into 12 equal parts. Shade 11 parts. Circle groups of 8 parts. Use the model to find 11/12 ÷ 2/3.

  2. The equation 1/4 × n = 1/2 represents equally sized portions. Find n, write the related division equation, and check.

  3. Draw a bar partitioned into 10 equal parts. Shade 10 parts. Circle groups of 6 parts. Use the model to find 1 ÷ 3/5.

  4. The equation 1/8 × n = 1 represents equally sized portions. Find n, write the related division equation, and check.

  5. Draw a bar partitioned into 8 equal parts. Shade 3 parts. Circle groups of 3 parts. Use the model to find 3/8 ÷ 3/8.

  6. The equation 1/2 × n = 1/3 represents equally sized portions. Find n, write the related division equation, and check.

  7. Draw a bar partitioned into 6 equal parts. Shade 5 parts. Circle groups of 4 parts. Use the model to find 5/6 ÷ 2/3. Explain the partial group.

  8. The equation 2/5 × n = 4/5 represents equally sized portions. Find n, write the related division equation, and check.

  9. Draw a bar partitioned into 8 equal parts. Shade 7 parts. Circle groups of 4 parts. Use the model to find 7/8 ÷ 1/2. Explain the partial group. Select one answer, then justify it with a model or calculation. A. 7/4 B. 11/4 C. 3/4 D. 35/2

  10. The equation 5/3 × n = 2 represents equally sized portions. Find n, write the related division equation, and check.

Answer key
  1. 11/8

    Each small part is 1/12; 11 shaded parts divided into groups of 8 gives 11/8 = 11/8 groups.

  2. n = 2; 1/2 ÷ 1/4 = 2

    1/2 ÷ 1/4 = 1/2 × 4 = 2. Check: 1/4 × 2 = 1/2.

  3. 5/3

    Each small part is 1/10; 10 shaded parts divided into groups of 6 gives 10/6 = 5/3 groups.

  4. n = 8; 1 ÷ 1/8 = 8

    1 ÷ 1/8 = 1 × 8 = 8. Check: 1/8 × 8 = 1.

  5. 1

    Each small part is 1/8; 3 shaded parts divided into groups of 3 gives 3/3 = 1 groups.

  6. n = 2/3; 1/3 ÷ 1/2 = 2/3

    1/3 ÷ 1/2 = 1/3 × 2 = 2/3. Check: 1/2 × 2/3 = 1/3.

  7. 5/4

    Each small part is 1/6; 5 shaded parts divided into groups of 4 gives 5/4 = 5/4 groups.

  8. n = 2; 4/5 ÷ 2/5 = 2

    4/5 ÷ 2/5 = 4/5 × 5/2 = 2. Check: 2/5 × 2 = 4/5.

  9. A - 7/4

    Each small part is 1/8; 7 shaded parts divided into groups of 4 gives 7/4 = 7/4 groups.

  10. n = 6/5; 2 ÷ 5/3 = 6/5

    2 ÷ 5/3 = 2 × 3/5 = 6/5. Check: 5/3 × 6/5 = 2.

Day 021 / Unit 2: Divide Fractions Using Reciprocals

6.NS.A.1

I can divide fractions and check using multiplication.

enVision Topic 1: Use Positive Rational Numbers | Spectrum companion Chapter 2

If (a/b) ÷ (c/d) = q, then (c/d) × q = a/b. Multiplying by d/c undoes multiplying by c/d, so divide by a nonzero fraction by multiplying by its reciprocal. Simplify the product.

Worked example

Calculate 1 ÷ 1/4. Simplify.

1 ÷ 1/4 = 1 × 4 = 4.

4

Worked example

Calculate 2 ÷ 4/3. Simplify and explain why your answer is reasonable. Select one answer, then justify it with a model or calculation. A. 1/2 B. 5/2 C. 2/3 D. 3/2

2 ÷ 4/3 = 2 × 3/4 = 3/2.

D - 3/2

  1. Calculate 3/4 ÷ 1/4. Simplify.

  2. Calculate 1/2 ÷ 1/4. Simplify.

  3. Calculate 1 ÷ 1/2. Simplify.

  4. Calculate 5/2 ÷ 1/4. Simplify.

  5. Calculate 4/3 ÷ 1/4. Simplify.

  6. Calculate 2 ÷ 4/3. Simplify.

  7. Calculate 1/2 ÷ 1/2. Simplify and explain why your answer is reasonable.

  8. Calculate 1 ÷ 1/2. Simplify and explain why your answer is reasonable. Select one answer, then justify it with a model or calculation. A. 3 B. 1/2 C. 2 D. 1

  9. Calculate 3/4 ÷ 1/5. Simplify and explain why your answer is reasonable. Select one answer, then justify it with a model or calculation. A. 15/4 B. 4/15 C. 11/4 D. 19/4

  10. Calculate 2/3 ÷ 1/2. Simplify and explain why your answer is reasonable.

Answer key
  1. 3

    3/4 ÷ 1/4 = 3/4 × 4 = 3.

  2. 2

    1/2 ÷ 1/4 = 1/2 × 4 = 2.

  3. 2

    1 ÷ 1/2 = 1 × 2 = 2.

  4. 10

    5/2 ÷ 1/4 = 5/2 × 4 = 10.

  5. 16/3

    4/3 ÷ 1/4 = 4/3 × 4 = 16/3.

  6. 3/2

    2 ÷ 4/3 = 2 × 3/4 = 3/2.

  7. 1

    1/2 ÷ 1/2 = 1/2 × 2 = 1.

  8. C - 2

    1 ÷ 1/2 = 1 × 2 = 2.

  9. A - 15/4

    3/4 ÷ 1/5 = 3/4 × 5 = 15/4.

  10. 4/3

    2/3 ÷ 1/2 = 2/3 × 2 = 4/3.

3/4 ÷ 1/2 = 3/4 × 2 = 3/2.

  9. Calculate 1 ÷ 1/8. Simplify and explain why your answer is reasonable. Select one answer, then justify it with a model or calculation. A. 7 B. 9 C. 1/8 D. 8

Day 026 / Unit 2: Write Fraction-Division Equations

6.NS.A.1

I can connect a fraction quotient to a missing factor.

enVision Topic 1: Use Positive Rational Numbers | Spectrum companion Chapter 2

Division and multiplication are inverse operations. For T ÷ s = n, the related equation is s × n = T. Label whether the unknown is the total, the group size, or the number of groups.

Worked example

The equation 1/4 × n = 3/4 represents equally sized portions. Find n, write the related division equation, and check.

3/4 ÷ 1/4 = 3/4 × 4 = 3. Check: 1/4 × 3 = 3/4.

n = 3; 3/4 ÷ 1/4 = 3

Worked example

The equation 1/5 × n = 3/8 represents equally sized portions. Find n, write the related division equation, and check.

3/8 ÷ 1/5 = 3/8 × 5 = 15/8. Check: 1/5 × 15/8 = 3/8.

n = 15/8; 3/8 ÷ 1/5 = 15/8

  1. The equation 1/2 × n = 3/4 represents equally sized portions. Find n, write the related division equation, and check.

  2. The equation 1/4 × n = 1/4 represents equally sized portions. Find n, write the related division equation, and check.

  3. The equation 1/4 × n = 1 represents equally sized portions. Find n, write the related division equation, and check.

  4. The equation 3/5 × n = 1/2 represents equally sized portions. Find n, write the related division equation, and check.

  5. The equation 1 × n = 7/8 represents equally sized portions. Find n, write the related division equation, and check.

  6. The equation 2 × n = 1 represents equally sized portions. Find n, write the related division equation, and check.

  7. The equation 1 × n = 1 represents equally sized portions. Find n, write the related division equation, and check.

  8. The equation 1/3 × n = 2/5 represents equally sized portions. Find n, write the related division equation, and check.

  9. The equation 5/4 × n = 2/3 represents equally sized portions. Find n, write the related division equation, and check.

  10. The equation 4/5 × n = 6/5 represents equally sized portions. Find n, write the related division equation, and check.

Answer key
  1. n = 3/2; 3/4 ÷ 1/2 = 3/2

    3/4 ÷ 1/2 = 3/4 × 2 = 3/2. Check: 1/2 × 3/2 = 3/4.

  2. n = 1; 1/4 ÷ 1/4 = 1

    1/4 ÷ 1/4 = 1/4 × 4 = 1. Check: 1/4 × 1 = 1/4.

  3. n = 4; 1 ÷ 1/4 = 4

    1 ÷ 1/4 = 1 × 4 = 4. Check: 1/4 × 4 = 1.

  4. n = 5/6; 1/2 ÷ 3/5 = 5/6

    1/2 ÷ 3/5 = 1/2 × 5/3 = 5/6. Check: 3/5 × 5/6 = 1/2.

  5. n = 7/8; 7/8 ÷ 1 = 7/8

    7/8 ÷ 1 = 7/8 × 1 = 7/8. Check: 1 × 7/8 = 7/8.

  6. n = 1/2; 1 ÷ 2 = 1/2

    1 ÷ 2 = 1 × 1/2 = 1/2. Check: 2 × 1/2 = 1.

  7. n = 1; 1 ÷ 1 = 1

    1 ÷ 1 = 1 × 1 = 1. Check: 1 × 1 = 1.

  8. n = 6/5; 2/5 ÷ 1/3 = 6/5

    2/5 ÷ 1/3 = 2/5 × 3 = 6/5. Check: 1/3 × 6/5 = 2/5.

  9. n = 8/15; 2/3 ÷ 5/4 = 8/15

    2/3 ÷ 5/4 = 2/3 × 4/5 = 8/15. Check: 5/4 × 8/15 = 2/3.

  10. n = 3/2; 6/5 ÷ 4/5 = 3/2

    6/5 ÷ 4/5 = 6/5 × 5/4 = 3/2. Check: 4/5 × 3/2 = 6/5.

Day 027 / Unit 2: Solve Fraction-Division Stories

6.NS.A.1

I can write and solve a fraction-division equation for a situation.

enVision Topic 1: Use Positive Rational Numbers | Spectrum companion Chapter 2

Identify the total amount and the size of one group. Use total ÷ group size for the number of groups. A quotient may include a partial group; if the question asks for full servings, use only the whole-number part.

1/2 ÷ 1/4 = 1/2 × 4 = 2.

Day 029 / Unit 2: Explain Fraction Models and Algorithms

6.NS.A.1

I can explain Fraction Models and Algorithms, and explain and check my reasoning.

enVision Topic 1: Use Positive Rational Numbers | Spectrum companion Chapter 2

A quotient asks how many groups of the divisor fit in the dividend. Rename both fractions with a common denominator, draw equal parts, and count groups. A partial group is a fraction of the divisor, not of the original whole. If (a/b) ÷ (c/d) = q, then (c/d) × q = a/b. Multiplying by d/c undoes multiplying by c/d, so divide by a nonzero fraction by multiplying by its reciprocal. Simplify the product. Rename a mixed number as (whole × denominator + numerator)/denominator. Keep the dividend, change division to multiplication, and use the reciprocal of the divisor. Simplify and interpret the result. Identify the total amount and the size of one group. Use total ÷ group size for the number of groups. A quotient may include a partial group; if the question asks for full servings, use only the whole-number part.

Worked example

Draw a bar partitioned into 12 equal parts. Shade 6 parts. Circle groups of 3 parts. Use the model to find 1/2 ÷ 1/4.

Each small part is 1/12; 6 shaded parts divided into groups of 3 gives 6/3 = 2 groups.

2

Worked example

  10. Calculate 2 ÷ 5/8. Simplify and explain why your answer is reasonable.

Answer key

    Each small part is 1/4; 3 shaded parts divided into groups of 2 gives 3/2 = 3/2 groups.

    Each small part is 1/8; 5 shaded parts divided into groups of 4 gives 5/4 = 5/4 groups.

Day 031 / Unit 3: Represent Signed Quantities and Zero

6.NS.C.5

I can use positive numbers, negative numbers, and zero in context.

enVision Topic 2: Integers and Rational Numbers | Spectrum companion Chapter 4

Choose a reference point for zero. Opposite directions or values use opposite signs: above sea level is positive and below sea level is negative. Zero means at the chosen reference, not always nothing.

Worked example

A marker is 32 m below sea level. Write its signed elevation and explain what elevation 0 means.

Sea level is the reference. Below is negative and above is positive.

-32 m; 0 means at sea level

Worked example

A sensor is 7 meters below a reference point. Select all correct interpretations. A. Its signed position is -7 m. B. Its signed position is +7 m. C. The opposite position is 7 m below the reference. D. A reading of 0 m means at the reference point.

Below the chosen zero is negative, so the position is -7. Zero identifies the reference; the opposite is 7 m above it.

A, D - Its signed position is -7 m.; A reading of 0 m means at the reference point.

Day 032 / Unit 3: Recognize Opposites

6.NS.C.6a

I can identify opposites and the opposite of an opposite.

enVision Topic 2: Integers and Rational Numbers | Spectrum companion Chapter 4

Opposites are equally far from zero on opposite sides of a number line. The opposite of 0 is 0. Reversing direction twice returns to the original number.

Worked example

Find the opposite of -2. Show both values on a number line.

-2 and 2 are each 2 units from 0 on opposite sides.

2

Worked example

Select all true statements about -15/2 and its opposite. Explain the role of zero. A. -15/2 and 15/2 are on the same side of zero. B. The opposite of the opposite of -15/2 is -15/2. C. The opposite of 0 is 1. D. The opposite of -15/2 is 15/2.

An opposite reverses direction without changing distance. Reversing twice returns to -15/2. Zero is its own opposite.

B, D - The opposite of the opposite of -15/2 is -15/2.; The opposite of -15/2 is 15/2.

  1. Find the opposite of -3. Show both values on a number line.

  2. Find the opposite of -6. Show both values on a number line.

  3. Find the opposite of -5. Show both values on a number line.

  4. Find the opposite of -4/5 and the opposite of that result. Locate all values on a number line.

  5. Find the opposite of -3/4 and the opposite of that result. Locate all values on a number line.

  6. Find the opposite of -2 and the opposite of that result. Locate all values on a number line.

  7. Find the opposite of -7 and the opposite of that result. Locate all values on a number line.

  8. Select all true statements about -5/2 and its opposite. Explain the role of zero. A. The opposite of the opposite of -5/2 is -5/2. B. The opposite of 0 is 1. C. -5/2 and 5/2 are on the same side of zero. D. The opposite of -5/2 is 5/2.

  9. Select all true statements about -7/2 and its opposite. Explain the role of zero. A. The opposite of the opposite of -7/2 is -7/2. B. The opposite of 0 is 1. C. -7/2 and 7/2 are on the same side of zero. D. The opposite of -7/2 is 7/2.

  10. Find the opposite of -7/5 and the opposite of that result. Locate all values on a number line.

Answer key
  1. 3

    -3 and 3 are each 3 units from 0 on opposite sides.

  2. 6

    -6 and 6 are each 6 units from 0 on opposite sides.

  3. 5

    -5 and 5 are each 5 units from 0 on opposite sides.

  4. 4/5; opposite again -4/5

    -4/5 and 4/5 are each 4/5 units from 0 on opposite sides.

  5. 3/4; opposite again -3/4

    -3/4 and 3/4 are each 3/4 units from 0 on opposite sides.

  6. 2; opposite again -2

    -2 and 2 are each 2 units from 0 on opposite sides.

  7. 7; opposite again -7

    -7 and 7 are each 7 units from 0 on opposite sides.

  8. A, D - The opposite of the opposite of -5/2 is -5/2.; The opposite of -5/2 is 5/2.

    An opposite reverses direction without changing distance. Reversing twice returns to -5/2. Zero is its own opposite.

  9. A, D - The opposite of the opposite of -7/2 is -7/2.; The opposite of -7/2 is 7/2.

    An opposite reverses direction without changing distance. Reversing twice returns to -7/2. Zero is its own opposite.

  10. 7/5; opposite again -7/5

    -7/5 and 7/5 are each 7/5 units from 0 on opposite sides.

Day 035 / Unit 3: Order Negative Fractions with Benchmarks

6.NS.C.6c, 6.NS.C.7a, 6.NS.C.7b

I can order Negative Fractions with Benchmarks, and explain and check my reasoning.

enVision Topic 2: Integers and Rational Numbers | Spectrum companion Chapter 4

Mark zero and equally spaced whole-number ticks. Partition each interval into equal parts for the denominator. Negative values are left of zero on a horizontal line and below zero on a vertical line. A number farther right is greater. Among negative numbers, the value closer to zero is greater. Use a common denominator or familiar benchmarks to compare negative fractions.

Worked example

Draw a horizontal or vertical number line. Plot and label -8, 0, and 6.

Negative -8 is left of or below 0; 6 is right of or above 0. Use equal intervals.

-8 < 0 < 6; unit steps

Worked example

Temperatures are -5 °C and -3 °C. Select all true comparisons. A. -5 is warmer than -3. B. -5 < -3 C. -3 lies to the right of -5 on a number line. D. -5 > 0

Among negatives, the number closer to zero is greater. Thus -3 is warmer and is farther right.

B, C - -5 < -3; -3 lies to the right of -5 on a number line.

  1. Draw a horizontal or vertical number line. Plot and label -8, 0, and 4.

  2. Compare -3 ___ -3 using <, >, or =. Sketch a number line to justify.

  3. Draw a horizontal or vertical number line. Plot and label -6, 0, and 2.

  4. Compare -2 ___ -1/2 using <, >, or =. Sketch a number line to justify.

  5. Draw a horizontal or vertical number line. Plot and label -2/3, 0, and 4/3.

  6. Compare -3/5 ___ -4/3 using <, >, or =. Sketch a number line to justify.

  7. Draw a horizontal or vertical number line. Plot and label -5/4, 0, and 3/4. Name the step size of your scale and explain the placement.

  8. Temperatures are -6 °C and -4 °C. Select all true comparisons. A. -6 is warmer than -4. B. -6 < -4 C. -4 lies to the right of -6 on a number line. D. -6 > 0

  9. On a number line, consecutive integer ticks are 1 unit apart. Plot -13/2, -6, and 0. Part A: name the leftmost point. Part B: give the midpoint between -7 and -6.

  10. Temperatures are -7/5°C and -2/5°C. Write an inequality comparing them and explain which is warmer.

Answer key
  1. -8 < 0 < 4; unit steps

    Negative -8 is left of or below 0; 4 is right of or above 0. Use equal intervals.

  2. =

    -3 = -3; greater numbers lie farther right.

  3. -6 < 0 < 2; unit steps

    Negative -6 is left of or below 0; 2 is right of or above 0. Use equal intervals.

  4. <

    -2 < -1/2; greater numbers lie farther right.

  5. -2/3 < 0 < 4/3; use steps of 1/3

    Negative -2/3 is left of or below 0; 4/3 is right of or above 0. Use equal intervals.

  6. >

    -3/5 > -4/3; greater numbers lie farther right.

  7. -5/4 < 0 < 3/4; use steps of 1/4

    Negative -5/4 is left of or below 0; 3/4 is right of or above 0. Use equal intervals.

  8. B, C - -6 < -4; -4 lies to the right of -6 on a number line.

    Among negatives, the number closer to zero is greater. Thus -4 is warmer and is farther right.

  9. Leftmost -13/2; midpoint -13/2

    -13/2 = -6 - 1/2 lies halfway between -7 and -6, left of both -6 and 0.

  10. -7/5 < -2/5; -2/5°C is warmer

    The greater value is farther right on a number line, even when both are negative.

Day 037 / Unit 3: Interpret Order in Context

6.NS.C.7a, 6.NS.C.7b, 6.NS.C.5

I can interpret Order in Context, and explain and check my reasoning.

enVision Topic 2: Integers and Rational Numbers | Spectrum companion Chapter 4

A number farther right is greater. Among negative numbers, the value closer to zero is greater. Use a common denominator or familiar benchmarks to compare negative fractions. Choose a reference point for zero. Opposite directions or values use opposite signs: above sea level is positive and below sea level is negative. Zero means at the chosen reference, not always nothing.

Worked example

Compare -7 ___ -6 using <, >, or =. Sketch a number line to justify.

-7 < -6; greater numbers lie farther right.

<

Worked example

A sensor is 5 meters below a reference point. Select all correct interpretations. A. Its signed position is +5 m. B. A reading of 0 m means at the reference point. C. The opposite position is 5 m below the reference. D. Its signed position is -5 m.

Below the chosen zero is negative, so the position is -5. Zero identifies the reference; the opposite is 5 m above it.

B, D - A reading of 0 m means at the reference point.; Its signed position is -5 m.

  1. Compare -5 ___ -2 using <, >, or =. Sketch a number line to justify.

  2. A marker is 12 m below sea level. Write its signed elevation and explain what elevation 0 means.

  3. Compare -3 ___ -4 using <, >, or =. Sketch a number line to justify.

  4. A marker is 32 m below sea level. Write its signed elevation and explain what elevation 0 means.

  5. Compare -4 ___ -1 using <, >, or =. Sketch a number line to justify.

  6. A marker is 8 m below sea level. Write its signed elevation and explain what elevation 0 means.

  7. Temperatures are -1/2°C and -1/5°C. Write an inequality comparing them and explain which is warmer.

  8. A sensor is 3 meters below a reference point. Select all correct interpretations. A. Its signed position is -3 m. B. A reading of 0 m means at the reference point. C. The opposite position is 3 m below the reference. D. Its signed position is +3 m.

  9. Temperatures are -6 °C and -4 °C. Select all true comparisons. A. -6 is warmer than -4. B. -6 < -4 C. -4 lies to the right of -6 on a number line. D. -6 > 0

  10. A marker is 42 m below sea level. Write its signed elevation and explain what elevation 0 means. Write the elevation of the point equally far on the opposite side of sea level.

Answer key
  1. <

    -5 < -2; greater numbers lie farther right.

  2. -12 m; 0 means at sea level

    Sea level is the reference. Below is negative and above is positive.

  3. >

    -3 > -4; greater numbers lie farther right.

  4. -32 m; 0 means at sea level

    Sea level is the reference. Below is negative and above is positive.

  5. <

    -4 < -1; greater numbers lie farther right.

  6. -8 m; 0 means at sea level

    Sea level is the reference. Below is negative and above is positive.

  7. -1/2 < -1/5; -1/5°C is warmer

    The greater value is farther right on a number line, even when both are negative.

  8. A, B - Its signed position is -3 m.; A reading of 0 m means at the reference point.

    Below the chosen zero is negative, so the position is -3. Zero identifies the reference; the opposite is 3 m above it.

  9. B, C - -6 < -4; -4 lies to the right of -6 on a number line.

    Among negatives, the number closer to zero is greater. Thus -4 is warmer and is farther right.

  10. -42 m; 0 means at sea level; opposite +42 m

    Sea level is the reference. Below is negative and above is positive.

Day 038 / Unit 3: Understand Absolute Value

6.NS.C.7c, 6.NS.C.7d

I can distinguish a number from its distance to zero.

enVision Topic 2: Integers and Rational Numbers | Spectrum companion Chapter 4

The absolute value |x| is the distance from x to zero, so it cannot be negative. A larger absolute value means farther from zero, but does not necessarily mean a greater number.

Worked example

Find |-36|. Explain the distance meaning.

-36 is 36 units from 0; distance is nonnegative.

36

Worked example

Consider -5 and 3. Select all true statements. A. |-5| = -5 B. |-5| = 5 C. -5 is farther from zero than 3. D. -5 > 3

Absolute value measures distance: 5 versus 3. A larger distance does not make the negative number greater.

B, C - |-5| = 5; -5 is farther from zero than 3.

  1. Find |-21|. Explain the distance meaning.

  2. Find |-14|. Explain the distance meaning.

  3. Find |-24|. Explain the distance meaning.

  4. Find |-9/2| and compare it with |3/2|.

  5. Find |-5| and compare it with |5/2|.

  6. Find |-3| and compare it with |1|.

  7. A diver is at -6 m and a bird is at 2 m relative to sea level. Who is farther from sea level? Who has greater elevation? Use absolute values.

  8. Consider -4 and 2. Select all true statements. A. |-4| = 4 B. -4 is farther from zero than 2. C. |-4| = -4 D. -4 > 2

  9. Consider -6 and 4. Select all true statements. A. -6 is farther from zero than 4. B. |-6| = -6 C. |-6| = 6 D. -6 > 4

  10. A diver is at -8 m and a bird is at 4 m relative to sea level. Who is farther from sea level? Who has greater elevation? Use absolute values.

Answer key
  1. 21

    -21 is 21 units from 0; distance is nonnegative.

  2. 14

    -14 is 14 units from 0; distance is nonnegative.

  3. 24

    -24 is 24 units from 0; distance is nonnegative.

  4. 9/2; 9/2 > 3/2

    -9/2 is 9/2 units from 0; distance is nonnegative.

  5. 5; 5 > 5/2

    -5 is 5 units from 0; distance is nonnegative.

  6. 3; 3 > 1

    -3 is 3 units from 0; distance is nonnegative.

  7. Diver: 6 m from sea level; bird has greater elevation

    |-6| = 6 > |2| = 2, but -6 < 2.

  8. A, B - |-4| = 4; -4 is farther from zero than 2.

    Absolute value measures distance: 4 versus 2. A larger distance does not make the negative number greater.

  9. A, C - -6 is farther from zero than 4.; |-6| = 6

    Absolute value measures distance: 6 versus 4. A larger distance does not make the negative number greater.

  10. Diver: 8 m from sea level; bird has greater elevation

    |-8| = 8 > |4| = 4, but -8 < 4.

Day 040 / Unit 3: Interpret Points on Axes and in Quadrants

6.NS.C.6b, 6.NS.C.6c

I can plot ordered pairs in all four quadrants.

enVision Topic 2: Integers and Rational Numbers | Spectrum companion Chapter 4

Start at the origin. Move horizontally by x, then vertically by y. Quadrant signs are I (+,+), II (-,+), III (-,-), IV (+,-). A point on an axis belongs to no quadrant.

Worked example

Plot P(6, -4) on a coordinate plane and name its quadrant.

Move 6 right, then 4 down.

Quadrant IV

Worked example

Plot P(-5, 4), Q(5, -4), and R(0, -4). Part A: name the quadrant of P and Q. Part B: explain why R is not in a quadrant.

P has signs (-,+), so II. Q has (+,-), so IV. R has x = 0 and is on an axis.

P: II; Q: IV; R is on the y-axis.

  1. Plot P(-7, -5) on a coordinate plane and name its quadrant.

  2. Plot P(5, -3) on a coordinate plane and name its quadrant.

  3. Plot P(-8, 2) on a coordinate plane and name its quadrant.

  4. Plot P(5, -6) on a coordinate plane and name its quadrant.

  5. Plot P(-8, -4) on a coordinate plane and name its quadrant.

  6. Plot P(3, 4) on a coordinate plane and name its quadrant.

  7. Plot P(-3/2, -1) on a coordinate plane and name its quadrant. Then plot Q on the y-axis at the same height and name its coordinates.

  8. Plot P(-4, 5), Q(4, -5), and R(0, -5). Part A: name the quadrant of P and Q. Part B: explain why R is not in a quadrant.

  9. Plot P(-4, 6), Q(4, -6), and R(0, -6). Part A: name the quadrant of P and Q. Part B: explain why R is not in a quadrant.

  10. Plot P(1, -3/2) on a coordinate plane and name its quadrant. Then plot Q on the y-axis at the same height and name its coordinates.

Answer key
  1. Quadrant III

    Move 7 left, then 5 down.

  2. Quadrant IV

    Move 5 right, then 3 down.

  3. Quadrant II

    Move 8 left, then 2 up.

  4. Quadrant IV

    Move 5 right, then 6 down.

  5. Quadrant III

    Move 8 left, then 4 down.

  6. Quadrant I

    Move 3 right, then 4 up.

  7. Quadrant III; Q(0, -1)

    Move 3/2 left, then 1 down.

  8. P: II; Q: IV; R is on the y-axis.

    P has signs (-,+), so II. Q has (+,-), so IV. R has x = 0 and is on an axis.

  9. P: II; Q: IV; R is on the y-axis.

    P has signs (-,+), so II. Q has (+,-), so IV. R has x = 0 and is on an axis.

  10. Quadrant IV; Q(0, -3/2)

    Move 1 right, then 3/2 down.

Day 042 / Unit 3: Reflect Points Across Axes

6.NS.C.6b

I can reflect points across an axis using signs.

enVision Topic 2: Integers and Rational Numbers | Spectrum companion Chapter 4

Across the x-axis, keep x and reverse the sign of y. Across the y-axis, reverse x and keep y. Across both axes, reverse both signs. The reflected point stays the same distance from the axis.

Worked example

Plot (-2, -7), reflect it across y-axis, and give the reflected coordinates.

Reverse the x sign: (2, -7).

(2, -7)

Worked example

The x-axis reflection changes y only; the y-axis reflection changes x only. Across both gives (4, -3).

B, D - Across the x-axis: (-4, -3).; Across the y-axis: (4, 3).

  1. Plot (-3, 4), reflect it across x-axis, and give the reflected coordinates.

  4. Plot (-7, -7), reflect it across y-axis, and give the reflected coordinates.

  6. Plot (-7, -3), reflect it across y-axis, and give the reflected coordinates.

  7. Plot (-8, 4), reflect it across both axes, and give the reflected coordinates.

  9. Point P is (-2, 2). Select all correct reflections. A. Across the x-axis: (2, 2). B. Across the x-axis: (-2, -2). C. Across the y-axis: (2, 2). D. Across both axes: (-2, 2).

  10. Plot (-8, -4), reflect it across both axes, and give the reflected coordinates.

Answer key

  4. (7, -7)

  5. (-4, -3)

    Reverse the y sign: (-4, -3).

  6. (7, -3)

    Reverse the x sign: (7, -3).

  7. (8, -4)

    Reverse both signs: (8, -4).

  8. A, C - Across the y-axis: (6, 4).; Across the x-axis: (-6, -4).

enVision Topic 2: Integers and Rational Numbers | Spectrum companion Chapter 4

Plot both points. If x-coordinates match, count the vertical units; if y-coordinates match, count horizontal units. On opposite sides of zero, add distances to zero. On the same side, subtract magnitudes.

Only one coordinate changes. Same side of 0: subtract magnitudes to get 4.

Day 045 / Unit 3: Unit 3 Test

6.NS.A.1, 6.NS.C.5, 6.NS.C.6a, 6.NS.C.6c, 6.NS.C.7a, 6.NS.C.7b, 6.NS.C.7c, 6.NS.C.7d, 6.NS.C.6b, 6.NS.C.8

I can choose a model, solve accurately, explain my reasoning, and check the result.

enVision Topic 2: Integers and Rational Numbers | Spectrum companion Chapter 4

  1. Calculate 3/4 × 1/2. Simplify.

  2. Calculate 3 1/4 × 1 1/3. Simplify.

  3. Draw a bar partitioned into 12 equal parts. Shade 11 parts. Circle groups of 7 parts. Use the model to find 11/12 ÷ 7/12.

  4. Draw a bar partitioned into 6 equal parts. Shade 6 parts. Circle groups of 3 parts. Use the model to find 1 ÷ 1/2.

  5. A marker is 8 m above sea level. Write its signed elevation and explain what elevation 0 means.

  6. Find the opposite of -8. Show both values on a number line.

  7. Draw a horizontal or vertical number line. Plot and label -3, 0, and 5.

  8. Draw a horizontal or vertical number line. Plot and label -8/3, 0, and 4/3.

  9. Draw a horizontal or vertical number line. Plot and label -1, 0, and 3/2.

  10. Compare -4/5 ___ -1 using <, >, or =. Sketch a number line to justify.

  11. Compare -4/5 ___ -7/10 using <, >, or =. Sketch a number line to justify.

  12. Consider -8 and 6. Select all true statements. A. -8 > 6 B. |-8| = -8 C. |-8| = 8 D. -8 is farther from zero than 6.

  13. Consider -5 and 3. Select all true statements. A. |-5| = -5 B. |-5| = 5 C. -5 is farther from zero than 3. D. -5 > 3

  14. Plot P(-2, 6), Q(2, -6), and R(0, -6). Part A: name the quadrant of P and Q. Part B: explain why R is not in a quadrant.

  15. Plot P(-3, 5), Q(3, -5), and R(0, -5). Part A: name the quadrant of P and Q. Part B: explain why R is not in a quadrant.

  16. Point P is (-6, 3). Select all correct reflections. A. Across the y-axis: (6, 3). B. Across the x-axis: (6, 3). C. Across both axes: (-6, 3). D. Across the x-axis: (-6, -3).

  17. Plot A(-5, -6), B(2, -6), and C(-5, 6). Part A: enter AB. Part B: enter AC and explain how zero helps find each distance.

  18. Point P is (-5, 5). Select all correct reflections. A. Across the x-axis: (-5, -5). B. Across the x-axis: (5, 5). C. Across both axes: (-5, 5). D. Across the y-axis: (5, 5).

  19. A sensor is 7 meters below a reference point. Select all correct interpretations. A. Its signed position is -7 m. B. Its signed position is +7 m. C. The opposite position is 7 m below the reference. D. A reading of 0 m means at the reference point.

  20. Select all true statements about -7/2 and its opposite. Explain the role of zero. A. The opposite of the opposite of -7/2 is -7/2. B. The opposite of 0 is 1. C. -7/2 and 7/2 are on the same side of zero. D. The opposite of -7/2 is 7/2.

Answer key
  1. 3/8

    3/4 × 1/2 = 3/8.

  2. 13/3

    13/4 × 4/3 = 13/3.

  3. 11/7

    Each small part is 1/12; 11 shaded parts divided into groups of 7 gives 11/7 = 11/7 groups.

  4. 2

    Each small part is 1/6; 6 shaded parts divided into groups of 3 gives 6/3 = 2 groups.

  5. +8 m; 0 means at sea level

    Sea level is the reference. Below is negative and above is positive.

  6. 8

    -8 and 8 are each 8 units from 0 on opposite sides.

  7. -3 < 0 < 5; unit steps

    Negative -3 is left of or below 0; 5 is right of or above 0. Use equal intervals.

  8. -8/3 < 0 < 4/3; use steps of 1/3

    Negative -8/3 is left of or below 0; 4/3 is right of or above 0. Use equal intervals.

  9. -1 < 0 < 3/2; use steps of 1/4

    Negative -1 is left of or below 0; 3/2 is right of or above 0. Use equal intervals.

  10. >

    -4/5 > -1; greater numbers lie farther right.

  11. <

    -4/5 < -7/10; greater numbers lie farther right.

  12. C, D - |-8| = 8; -8 is farther from zero than 6.

    Absolute value measures distance: 8 versus 6. A larger distance does not make the negative number greater.

  13. B, C - |-5| = 5; -5 is farther from zero than 3.

    Absolute value measures distance: 5 versus 3. A larger distance does not make the negative number greater.

  14. P: II; Q: IV; R is on the y-axis.

    P has signs (-,+), so II. Q has (+,-), so IV. R has x = 0 and is on an axis.

  15. P: II; Q: IV; R is on the y-axis.

    P has signs (-,+), so II. Q has (+,-), so IV. R has x = 0 and is on an axis.

  16. A, D - Across the y-axis: (6, 3).; Across the x-axis: (-6, -3).

    The x-axis reflection changes y only; the y-axis reflection changes x only. Across both gives (6, -3).

  17. AB = 7 units; AC = 12 units

    AB is horizontal: 5 + 2 = 7. AC is vertical: 6 + 6 = 12. Each segment crosses an axis.

  18. A, D - Across the x-axis: (-5, -5).; Across the y-axis: (5, 5).

    The x-axis reflection changes y only; the y-axis reflection changes x only. Across both gives (5, -5).

  19. A, D - Its signed position is -7 m.; A reading of 0 m means at the reference point.

    Below the chosen zero is negative, so the position is -7. Zero identifies the reference; the opposite is 7 m above it.

  20. A, D - The opposite of the opposite of -7/2 is -7/2.; The opposite of -7/2 is 7/2.

    An opposite reverses direction without changing distance. Reversing twice returns to -7/2. Zero is its own opposite.

Day 046 / Unit 4: Write and Evaluate Powers

6.EE.A.1

I can write repeated multiplication using a whole-number exponent.

enVision Topic 3: Numeric and Algebraic Expressions | Spectrum companion Chapter 5

The base is the repeated factor and the exponent tells how many copies to multiply. For a positive exponent, 4^3 = 4 × 4 × 4, not 4 × 3. Parentheses show when a whole product is the base.

Worked example

Write 3^2 as repeated multiplication and evaluate.

The exponent counts 2 factors of 3; multiply all factors.

3 × 3 = 9

Worked example

Select all expressions equal to 2³. A. 9 B. 2 × 3 C. 8 D. 2 × 2 × 2

The exponent 3 means three factors of 2; their product is 8. It does not mean 2 times 3.

C, D - 8; 2 × 2 × 2

  1. Write 3^3 as repeated multiplication and evaluate.

  2. Write 2^4 as repeated multiplication and evaluate.

  3. Write 4^3 as repeated multiplication and evaluate.

  4. Write 6^4 as repeated multiplication and evaluate.

  5. Write 7^2 as repeated multiplication and evaluate.

  6. Write 2^2 as repeated multiplication and evaluate.

  7. Write 4^3 as repeated multiplication and evaluate. Explain why it differs from 4 × 3.

  8. Select all expressions equal to 3³. A. 3 × 3 B. 27 C. 28 D. 3 × 3 × 3

  9. Select all expressions equal to 6³. A. 6 × 3 B. 6 × 6 × 6 C. 217 D. 216

  10. Write 3^3 as repeated multiplication and evaluate. Explain why it differs from 3 × 3.

Answer key
  1. 3 × 3 × 3 = 27

    The exponent counts 3 factors of 3; multiply all factors.

  2. 2 × 2 × 2 × 2 = 16

    The exponent counts 4 factors of 2; multiply all factors.

  3. 4 × 4 × 4 = 64

    The exponent counts 3 factors of 4; multiply all factors.

  4. 6 × 6 × 6 × 6 = 1296

    The exponent counts 4 factors of 6; multiply all factors.

  5. 7 × 7 = 49

    The exponent counts 2 factors of 7; multiply all factors.

  6. 2 × 2 = 4

    The exponent counts 2 factors of 2; multiply all factors.

  7. 4 × 4 × 4 = 64; 4 × 3 = 12

    The exponent counts 3 factors of 4; multiply all factors.

  8. B, D - 27; 3 × 3 × 3

    The exponent 3 means three factors of 3; their product is 27. It does not mean 3 times 3.

  9. B, D - 6 × 6 × 6; 216

    The exponent 3 means three factors of 6; their product is 216. It does not mean 6 times 3.

  10. 3 × 3 × 3 = 27; 3 × 3 = 9

    The exponent counts 3 factors of 3; multiply all factors.

enVision Topic 3: Numeric and Algebraic Expressions | Spectrum companion Chapter 5

22

Day 048 / Unit 4: Find Greatest Common Factors

6.NS.B.4a

I can find the greatest common factor and interpret equal groups.

enVision Topic 3: Numeric and Algebraic Expressions | Spectrum companion Chapter 1

List factor pairs or use prime factorizations. The greatest factor shared by both numbers is the GCF. For identical kits with no leftovers, the GCF gives the greatest number of kits.

Worked example

Find the GCF of 6 and 9. Show factor pairs or prime factors.

Common factors: 1, 3; greatest is 3.

3

Worked example

Select all true statements about 36 and 54. Explain why the greatest factor fits the kit situation. A. 90 divides both counts. B. 19 is their greatest common factor. C. 18 divides both 36 and 54. D. 18 is the largest number of identical no-leftover kits from 36 pencils and 54 erasers.

GCF(36, 54) = 18. Divide each count by 18: 2 pencils and 3 erasers per kit. A larger shared divisor is impossible.

C, D - 18 divides both 36 and 54.; 18 is the largest number of identical no-leftover kits from 36 pencils and 54 erasers.

  9. Select all true statements about 30 and 45. Explain why the greatest factor fits the kit situation. A. 15 is the largest number of identical no-leftover kits from 30 pencils and 45 erasers. B. 15 divides both 30 and 45. C. 16 is their greatest common factor. D. 75 divides both counts.

  10. Make the greatest possible number of identical kits from 18 pencils and 30 erasers with no leftovers. Find the number of kits and each kit’s contents.

Answer key
  1. 4

    Common factors: 1, 2, 4; greatest is 4.

  2. 5

    Common factors: 1, 5; greatest is 5.

  3. 4

    Common factors: 1, 2, 4; greatest is 4.

  4. 15

  5. 10

    Common factors: 1, 2, 5, 10; greatest is 10.

  6. 14

    Common factors: 1, 2, 7, 14; greatest is 14.

  7. 12 kits; 2 pencils and 3 erasers each

    GCF(24, 36) = 12; divide both counts by 12.

  8. A, C - 9 is the largest number of identical no-leftover kits from 18 pencils and 27 erasers.; 9 divides both 18 and 27.

Day 049 / Unit 4: Find Least Common Multiples

6.NS.B.4b

I can find the least common multiple of two numbers no greater than 12.

enVision Topic 3: Numeric and Algebraic Expressions | Spectrum companion Chapter 1

List positive multiples of both numbers. The smallest shared multiple is the LCM. Repeating events that begin together next occur together after this many time units.

Worked example

Find the LCM of 2 and 3 by listing multiples.

The first positive multiple shared by 2 and 3 is 6.

6

Worked example

List multiples or use prime factors. The least shared positive multiple is 18; multiples of 18 are later shared times.

B, C - They next flash together after 18 seconds.; 36 seconds is also a time they flash together.

  9. Two signals flash together now and then every 10 and 9 seconds. Select all true statements. A. They next flash together after 90 seconds. B. They next flash together after 1 seconds. C. 180 seconds is also a time they flash together. D. The next shared time is 91 seconds.
