  18. A frequency table records books read: 0 books: 1 students; 1: 3; 2: 4; 3: 5. Part A: draw a dot plot. Part B: enter the observation count and explain how to verify it.

  19. A class studies a collection of students. Select all statistical questions, and identify the expected variability. A. How many centimeters are in 2 meters? B. What is the reading time in minutes of each of the students? C. What is 2 + 5? D. How does reading time vary among the students?

  20. Six students recorded reading times (minutes): 6, 8, 8, 10, 10, 12. Select all true statements. A. The median is the average of the 3rd and 4th ordered values. B. The median is 10 minutes. C. The median is always the largest observation. D. The median is 9 minutes.

Answer key
  1. 16 cm²

    A = bh/2 = 8 × 4 ÷ 2 = 16. Two such triangles make a parallelogram.

  2. 6 m²

    Cut and shift a corner; A = bh = 2 × 3 = 6.

  3. 32 m²

    Outer area 36 minus cutout 4 = 32.

  4. Sides 9 and 9; area 81 square units

    Horizontal length 9 - (0) = 9; vertical length 9 - (0) = 9; area = 9 × 9.

  5. Yes; plant heights vary.

    Look for a collection of data and anticipated variation; identify the population and variable.

  6. n = 6; minutes; mean 9/2 min

    Sum = 27; mean = 27 ÷ 6 = 9/2.

  7. 8: 1 dot; 9: 2 dots; 10: 2 dots; 11: 1 dot; n = 6

    Each observation contributes one dot; stack repeated values at their number-line position.

  8. (7, 9, 10, 11, 13)

    Box runs from 9 to 11, median line at 10, whiskers to 7 and 13.

  9. 6: 1 dot; 10: 2 dots; 14: 2 dots; 18: 1 dot; n = 6

    Each observation contributes one dot; stack repeated values at their number-line position.

  10. Frequencies 0, 3, 4, 3

    Count every value once. Bars touch and have equal width; horizontal axis is minutes, vertical axis is frequency.

  11. Q1 11, Q3 17; IQR 6 min; range 18 min

    Lower half [5, 11, 11] has median 11; upper half [17, 17, 23] has median 17. Subtract Q1 from Q3.

  12. Mean 13/2 min; MAD 5/2 min

    Mean = 39/6 = 13/2. Absolute deviations: 9/2, 3/2, 3/2, 3/2, 3/2, 9/2. Their sum divided by 6 is 5/2. This is the average distance from the mean.

  13. 6 observations; minutes; median 8, IQR 2; mean 8, MAD 5/3. Roughly balanced; mean/MAD or median/IQR with justification.

    Order the observations. Middle positions give median 8 and half medians give IQR 2. Mean is 48/6; averaging distances gives MAD 5/3. Tie the chosen summary to the displayed shape and context.

  14. 6 observations; minutes; median 16, IQR 6; mean 16, MAD 5. Roughly balanced; mean/MAD or median/IQR with justification.

    Order the observations. Middle positions give median 16 and half medians give IQR 6. Mean is 96/6; averaging distances gives MAD 5. Tie the chosen summary to the displayed shape and context.

  15. 6 observations; minutes; median 14, IQR 6; mean 14, MAD 5. Roughly balanced; mean/MAD or median/IQR with justification.

    Order the observations. Middle positions give median 14 and half medians give IQR 6. Mean is 84/6; averaging distances gives MAD 5. Tie the chosen summary to the displayed shape and context.

  16. 6 observations; minutes; median 27/2, IQR 5; mean 27/2, MAD 25/6. Roughly balanced; mean/MAD or median/IQR with justification.

    Order the observations. Middle positions give median 27/2 and half medians give IQR 5. Mean is 81/6; averaging distances gives MAD 25/6. Tie the chosen summary to the displayed shape and context.

  17. First median 9; second 13 min; both IQR 2 min.

    Adding 4 shifts every position and both quartiles by 4. The median increases by 4; Q3 - Q1 remains 2.

  18. 13 students; stacks at 0, 1, 2, 3 have 1, 3, 4, 5 dots.

    Place one dot per student, not one per distinct value. Total dots = 1 + 3 + 4 + 5 = 13. Label the number line books read.

  19. B, D - What is the reading time in minutes of each of the students?; How does reading time vary among the students?

    Different students can have different reading time. The questions anticipate variation across a collection. The conversion and arithmetic questions have fixed answers.

  20. A, D - The median is the average of the 3rd and 4th ordered values.; The median is 9 minutes.

    Six ordered observations have middle positions 3 and 4, so median = (8 + 10)/2 = 9.

Day 136 / Unit 10: Connect Rational Numbers, Expressions, and Ratios

6.NS.A.1, 6.NS.C.7a, 6.NS.C.7b, 6.EE.A.2c, 6.RP.A.3a

I can connect Rational Numbers, Expressions, and Ratios, and explain and check my reasoning.

Cumulative application across enVision Topics 1–8 | Spectrum cross-chapter review

If (a/b) ÷ (c/d) = q, then (c/d) × q = a/b. Multiplying by d/c undoes multiplying by c/d, so divide by a nonzero fraction by multiplying by its reciprocal. Simplify the product. A number farther right is greater. Among negative numbers, the value closer to zero is greater. Use a common denominator or familiar benchmarks to compare negative fractions. Replace every variable with its assigned value, using parentheses. Apply the order of operations. In formulas, label the answer with the appropriate measurement units. Multiply both values in a ratio by the same scale factor. Organize pairs in a table and check that every row compares the same quantities in the same order.

Worked example

Calculate 1/2 ÷ 1/2. Simplify.

1/2 ÷ 1/2 = 1/2 × 2 = 1.

1

Worked example

A recipe uses 5 cups oats for 3 cups seeds. Select all (oats, seeds) pairs that keep this ratio. A. (26, 15) B. (15, 9) C. (10, 7) D. (10, 6)

Equivalent ratios multiply both quantities by the same factor. A uses 2; B uses 3. C and D change only one scaled quantity.

B, D - (15, 9); (10, 6)

  1. Calculate 1/4 ÷ 1/2. Simplify.

  2. Compare -2 ___ -7 using <, >, or =. Sketch a number line to justify.

  3. Evaluate 4x + 3 for x = 4.

  4. A mix uses 3 cups of oats for 7 cups of seeds. Complete the table (oats, seeds): (3, 7), (6, ___), (12, ___).

  5. Calculate 1/2 ÷ 1. Simplify.

  6. Compare -3/2 ___ -7/5 using <, >, or =. Sketch a number line to justify.

  7. A rectangle has length x + 8 and width 2. Use A = lw to write its area expression and find A when x = 5.

  8. A recipe uses 3 cups oats for 4 cups seeds. Select all (oats, seeds) pairs that keep this ratio. A. (9, 12) B. (6, 9) C. (6, 8) D. (16, 20)

  9. Calculate 2/3 ÷ 1/5. Simplify and explain why your answer is reasonable. Select one answer, then justify it with a model or calculation. A. 10/3 B. 7/3 C. 3/10 D. 13/3

  10. Temperatures are -7/3°C and -5/2°C. Write an inequality comparing them and explain which is warmer.

Answer key
  1. 1/2

    1/4 ÷ 1/2 = 1/4 × 2 = 1/2.

  2. >

    -2 > -7; greater numbers lie farther right.

  3. 19

    Substitute first: 4 × 4 + 3 = 19.

  4. 14; 28

    Multiply both parts by 2 and by 4.

  5. 1/2

    1/2 ÷ 1 = 1/2 × 1 = 1/2.

  6. <

    -3/2 < -7/5; greater numbers lie farther right.

  7. A = 2(x + 8); 26 square units

    Substitute x = 5: 2(5 + 8) = 26.

  8. A, C - (9, 12); (6, 8)

    Equivalent ratios multiply both quantities by the same factor. A uses 2; B uses 3. C and D change only one scaled quantity.

  9. A - 10/3

    2/3 ÷ 1/5 = 2/3 × 5 = 10/3.

  10. -7/3 > -5/2; -7/3°C is warmer

    The greater value is farther right on a number line, even when both are negative.

Day 137 / Unit 10: Connect Equations, Rates, and Percent

6.EE.B.6, 6.EE.B.7, 6.RP.A.3b, 6.RP.A.3c

I can connect Equations, Rates, and Percent, and explain and check my reasoning.

Cumulative application across enVision Topics 1–8 | Spectrum cross-chapter review

Define the unknown quantity. Translate the relationship into an equation before calculating. Use the inverse operation and check whether the answer makes sense in the situation. At a constant speed, distance = speed × time. Divide distance by time to find speed, or distance by speed to find time. Keep units consistent before calculating. Write part = decimal rate × whole. Divide the part by the decimal rate to find the whole. Check by taking the stated percent of your answer. Divide the part by the whole, then multiply by 100. Keep the same units in part and whole. The percent can be above 100 if the part is larger than the reference whole.

Worked example

A collection grows by 5 cards to 10 cards. Let x be the starting count. Write and solve an equation.

Subtract 5 from both sides; 5 + 5 = 10.

x + 5 = 10; x = 5 cards

Worked example

A quantity changes from a reference whole of 120 to a compared amount of 30. Part A: the compared amount is what percent of the reference whole? Part B: explain which number belongs in the denominator.

Percent = (30 ÷ 120) × 100 = 25%. The denominator is the reference whole, not automatically the larger number.

25%; denominator 120

  1. A collection grows by 5 cards to 35 cards. Let x be the starting count. Write and solve an equation.

  2. A bus travels at 40 km/h. How long does 200 km take?

  3. 50% of a number is 40. Find the number and check.

  4. 144 is what percent of 240?

  5. 7 identical lengths of rope total 28 m. Let x be the length of one piece. Write and solve an equation, then check.

  6. A bus travels at 40 km/h. How long does 120 km take?

  7. 125% of a number is 225. Find the number and check.

  8. A quantity changes from a reference whole of 100 to a compared amount of 75. Part A: the compared amount is what percent of the reference whole? Part B: explain which number belongs in the denominator.

  9. 2 identical ropes have total length 3 meters. Part A: write and solve an equation for one rope length x. Part B: show a substitution check.

  10. A bus travels at 35 km/h. How long does 175/2 km take?

Answer key
  1. x + 5 = 35; x = 30 cards

    Subtract 5 from both sides; 30 + 5 = 35.

  2. 5 hours

    Time = distance ÷ speed = 200 ÷ 40 = 5.

  3. 80

    Whole = 40 ÷ 1/2 = 80. Check 50% × 80 = 40.

  4. 60%

    144 ÷ 240 × 100 = 60%; the reference whole is 240.

  5. 7x = 28; x = 4 m

    Divide the total by 7: 28 ÷ 7 = 4.

  6. 3 hours

    Time = distance ÷ speed = 120 ÷ 40 = 3.

  7. 180

    Whole = 225 ÷ 5/4 = 180. Check 125% × 180 = 225.

  8. 75%; denominator 100

    Percent = (75 ÷ 100) × 100 = 75%. The denominator is the reference whole, not automatically the larger number.

  9. 2x = 3; x = 3/2 meters

    Total = number of ropes × one length. Divide both sides by 2; 3 ÷ 2 = 3/2. Check 2 × 3/2 = 3.

  10. 5/2 hours

    Time = distance ÷ speed = 175/2 ÷ 35 = 5/2.

Day 138 / Unit 10: Connect Coordinates and Geometry Models

6.NS.C.8, 6.G.A.3, 6.G.A.1, 6.G.A.4, 6.G.A.2

I can connect Coordinates and Geometry Models, and explain and check my reasoning.

Cumulative application across enVision Topics 1–8 | Spectrum cross-chapter review

Plot both points. If x-coordinates match, count the vertical units; if y-coordinates match, count horizontal units. On opposite sides of zero, add distances to zero. On the same side, subtract magnitudes. Plot vertices in order and connect them, including the last to the first. Equal x-values make a vertical side; equal y-values make a horizontal side. Count or subtract coordinates to get the side lengths. A closed rectangular prism has two faces of area lw, two of lh, and two of wh. Add the six face areas: SA = 2lw + 2lh + 2wh. If a face is missing, do not include it. A rectangular prism has volume V = lwh = Bh, where B is base area. Fractional edge lengths still multiply. Use cubic units; surface area and volume measure different attributes.

Worked example

Plot (-3, 5) and (-8, 5). Find their distance.

Only one coordinate changes. Same side of 0: subtract magnitudes to get 5.

5 units

Worked example

A prism measures 13/2 m by 2 m by 2 m. Part A: enter its volume. Part B: how many cubes with edge 1/2 m fill it? Explain why the cube count differs from the cubic-meter volume.

Multiply edges: 13/2 × 2 × 2 = 26. The edge counts are 13, 4, 4, giving 208 cubes. Each cube is 1/8 m³; 208/8 = 26.

26 m³; 208 cubes

  1. Plot (4, -6) and (4, -10). Find their distance.

  2. Plot and connect (0,0), (12,0), (12,8), (0,8). Find the side lengths and area.

  3. A 5 cm by 5 cm base and 6 cm height form a closed box. Draw its net and find the material area. Ignore tabs and overlap.

  4. A rectangular prism has edges 3, 3, and 3 cm. Sketch it and find its volume.

  5. Plot (6, -4) and (6, 5). Find their distance.

  6. Plot and connect (-6,-4), (4,-4), (4,4), (-6,4). Find the side lengths and area.

  7. A 4 cm by 2 cm base and 5 cm height form an open-top box. Draw its net and find the material area. Ignore tabs and overlap.

  8. A prism measures 5/2 m by 1 m by 5/2 m. Part A: enter its volume. Part B: how many cubes with edge 1/2 m fill it? Explain why the cube count differs from the cubic-meter volume.

  9. Plot A(-7, -6), B(5, -6), and C(-7, 6). Part A: enter AB. Part B: enter AC and explain how zero helps find each distance.

  10. Plot and connect (-3,-6), (3,-6), (3,3), (-3,3). Find the side lengths and area. Find the perimeter too.

Answer key
  1. 4 units

    Only one coordinate changes. Same side of 0: subtract magnitudes to get 4.

  2. Sides 12 and 8; area 96 square units

    Horizontal length 12 - (0) = 12; vertical length 8 - (0) = 8; area = 12 × 8.

  3. 170 cm²

    Closed surface area = 2(25+30+30) = 170.

  4. 27 cm³

    B = 3 × 3 = 9 cm²; V = B × 3 = 27 cm³.

  5. 9 units

    Only one coordinate changes. Opposite sides of 0: 4 + 5 = 9.

  6. Sides 10 and 8; area 80 square units

    Horizontal length 4 - (-6) = 10; vertical length 4 - (-4) = 8; area = 10 × 8.

  7. 68 cm²

    Closed surface area = 2(8+20+10) = 76. Remove the top area 8: 68.

  8. 25/4 m³; 50 cubes

    Multiply edges: 5/2 × 1 × 5/2 = 25/4. The edge counts are 5, 2, 5, giving 50 cubes. Each cube is 1/8 m³; 50/8 = 25/4.

  9. AB = 12 units; AC = 12 units

    AB is horizontal: 7 + 5 = 12. AC is vertical: 6 + 6 = 12. Each segment crosses an axis.

  10. Sides 6 and 9; area 54 square units; perimeter 30 units

    Horizontal length 3 - (-3) = 6; vertical length 3 - (-6) = 9; area = 6 × 9.

Day 139 / Unit 10: Explain Data and Critique Mathematical Claims

6.SP.B.5a, 6.SP.B.5b, 6.SP.B.5c, 6.SP.B.5d, 6.EE.A.4, 6.EE.B.5, 6.EE.B.8

I can explain Data and Critique Mathematical Claims, and explain and check my reasoning.

Cumulative application across enVision Topics 1–8 | Spectrum cross-chapter review

A useful summary names the group and attribute, units, observation count, center, variability, and shape. Explain why the chosen center and spread fit the data and mention any striking value. Use operation properties to rewrite an expression. Matching values at one input do not prove equivalence for all inputs. A single counterexample is enough to show two expressions are not equivalent. Substitute each value and decide whether the comparison is true. A continuous number-line ray represents infinitely many real-number solutions. A real situation may restrict useful solutions to whole numbers. Find the mean. Calculate each value’s distance from the mean, add those distances, then divide by the number of observations. MAD describes a typical distance from the mean in the original units.

Worked example

Reading times in minutes are 6, 7, 7, 8, 8, 9. Write a summary with count, attribute, units, center, variability, shape, and a justified choice of measures.

Calculate the center and spread before interpreting them in context. Other accurate, justified summaries are acceptable.

6 observations of reading time in minutes. Mean 15/2 min, MAD 5/6 min; roughly symmetric, so mean/MAD are reasonable.

Worked example

Six students recorded reading times (minutes): 3, 8, 8, 13, 13, 18. Part A: enter the mean. Part B: enter the MAD and interpret it in minutes.

Mean = 63/6 = 21/2. Absolute deviations: 15/2, 5/2, 5/2, 5/2, 5/2, 15/2. Their sum divided by 6 is 25/6. This is the average distance from the mean.

Mean 21/2 min; MAD 25/6 min

  1. Reading times in minutes are 2, 3, 3, 4, 4, 5. Write a summary with count, attribute, units, center, variability, shape, and a justified choice of measures.

  2. Are 5(x + 3) and 5x + 15 equivalent for every x? Use a property or a counterexample.

  3. Which values in {9, 10, 11} satisfy x < 10? Test each by substitution.

  4. Reading times in minutes are 7, 10, 10, 13, 13, 16. Calculate the mean and MAD. Show all absolute deviations.

  5. Reading times in minutes are 5, 7, 7, 9, 9, 11. Write a summary with count, attribute, units, center, variability, shape, and a justified choice of measures.

  6. Are 6(x + 2) and 6x + 12 equivalent for every x? Use a property or a counterexample.

  7. Which values in {10, 11, 12} satisfy x < 11? Does the real-number inequality have only these solutions? Explain.

  8. Six students recorded reading times (minutes): 3, 7, 7, 11, 11, 15. Part A: enter the mean. Part B: enter the MAD and interpret it in minutes.

  9. Six students recorded reading times (minutes): 5, 11, 11, 17, 17, 31. Part A: describe center and spread using appropriate measures. Part B: describe shape, count, units, and any striking value; justify the chosen measures.

  10. Are 3(x + 4) and 3x + 12 equivalent for every x? Use a property or a counterexample.

Answer key
  1. 6 observations of reading time in minutes. Mean 7/2 min, MAD 5/6 min; roughly symmetric, so mean/MAD are reasonable.

    Calculate the center and spread before interpreting them in context. Other accurate, justified summaries are acceptable.

  2. Yes; distribution gives 5x + 15

    5(x + 3) = 5x + 15. This matches.

  3. 9

    Values below 10 work; the endpoint does not work.

  4. Mean 23/2 min; MAD 5/2 min

    Distances: 9/2, 3/2, 3/2, 3/2, 3/2, 9/2; sum 15; divide by 6 = 5/2.

  5. 6 observations of reading time in minutes. Mean 8 min, MAD 5/3 min; roughly symmetric, so mean/MAD are reasonable.

    Calculate the center and spread before interpreting them in context. Other accurate, justified summaries are acceptable.

  6. Yes; distribution gives 6x + 12

    6(x + 2) = 6x + 12. This matches.

  7. 10; no, infinitely many real values satisfy it

    Values below 11 work; the endpoint does not work.

  8. Mean 9 min; MAD 10/3 min

    Mean = 54/6 = 9. Absolute deviations: 6, 2, 2, 2, 2, 6. Their sum divided by 6 is 10/3. This is the average distance from the mean.

  9. 6 observations; minutes; median 14, IQR 6; mean 46/3, MAD 19/3. A high value makes median/IQR a useful choice.

    Order the observations. Middle positions give median 14 and half medians give IQR 6. Mean is 92/6; averaging distances gives MAD 19/3. Tie the chosen summary to the displayed shape and context.

  10. Yes; distribution gives 3x + 12

    3(x + 4) = 3x + 12. This matches.

  19. 5 notebooks cost $25. Select all statements at the same price per notebook. A. 3 notebooks cost $16. B. Each notebook costs $125. C. 3 notebooks cost $15. D. Each notebook costs $5.

  20. Select all expressions equivalent to 2(x + 4). A. 2x + 4 B. 2x + 8 C. 8 + 2x D. 6x

  21. Calculate 7/4 ÷ 3/5. Simplify. Select one answer, then justify it with a model or calculation. A. 12/35 B. 23/12 C. 47/12 D. 35/12

  22. A 10 m by 10 m rectangle has a right-triangle corner removed with perpendicular legs 4 m and 6 m. Part A: enter the removed area. Part B: enter the remaining area and justify subtraction.

  23. Select all expressions equivalent to 5x + 6x + 5. A. 16x B. 11x + 6 C. (5 + 6)x + 5 D. 11x + 5

  24. Calculate 5 ÷ 2 1/2. Simplify. Select one answer, then justify it with a model or calculation. A. 2 B. 3 C. 1 D. 1/2

  25. Store A sells 6 notebooks for $24. Store B sells 7 notebooks for $35. Part A: enter the unit price at each store. Part B: choose the better buy and explain why package totals alone are insufficient.

  26. Select all expressions equivalent to 6(x + 4). A. 10x B. 6x + 24 C. 6x + 4 D. 24 + 6x

  27. Select all true statements about 12 and 18. Explain why the greatest factor fits the kit situation. A. 6 is the largest number of identical no-leftover kits from 12 pencils and 18 erasers. B. 30 divides both counts. C. 7 is their greatest common factor. D. 6 divides both 12 and 18.

  28. Six students recorded reading times (minutes): 5, 8, 8, 11, 11, 14. Part A: draw a box plot and label its five-number summary. Part B: enter the IQR.

  29. Solve x - 5/2 = 2. Select every representation of the solution. A. 18/4 B. 9/2 C. 11/2 D. 13/2

  30. A bicycle travels 40 km in 2 hours at constant speed. Select all true statements. A. The speed is 42 km per hour. B. In 2 hours it travels 40 km. C. In 1 hour it travels 21 km. D. The speed is 20 km per hour.

  31. Temperatures are -9 °C and -7 °C. Select all true comparisons. A. -9 is warmer than -7. B. -9 < -7 C. -7 lies to the right of -9 on a number line. D. -9 > 0

  32. Solve 2 × x = 7. Select every representation of the solution. A. 14/4 B. 9/2 C. 11/2 D. 7/2

  33. Use 1 feet = 12 inches. Select all statements equivalent to 7/2 feet. A. 42 inches B. 43 inches C. 7/2 × 12 inches D. 7/24 inches

  34. Consider -9 and 7. Select all true statements. A. -9 is farther from zero than 7. B. |-9| = -9 C. -9 > 7 D. |-9| = 9

  35. Solve x ÷ 3 = 13/6. Select every representation of the solution. A. 26/4 B. 17/2 C. 13/2 D. 15/2

  36. A school club has 80 members; 15% take part in a project. Part A: enter the number of participants. Part B: write an equation and explain whether the answer is greater or less than the whole.

  37. A closed box is 6 cm by 2 cm by 3 cm. Part A: enter its surface area. Part B: the 6-by-2 top is removed; enter the remaining surface area and explain.

  38. Reading-time frequencies (minutes) are 0–4: 3, 5–9: 4, 10–14: 5, 15–19: 4. Part A: draw a histogram. Part B: enter the observation count and name every highest-frequency interval.

  39. A jug contains 2 L. Each full bottle needs 4/3 L. How many full bottles can be filled, and how much liquid remains?

  40. Select all values of x from the list that make x + 2 > 5 true. Explain why each rejected value fails. A. 5 B. 3 C. 2 D. 4

  41. 75 is 125% of an unknown quantity w. Select all equations that find w. A. (125/100)w = 75 B. w = 75 + 125 C. w = 75 ÷ (125/100) D. w = 75 × (125/100)

  42. Plot P(-7, 4), Q(7, -4), and R(0, -4). Part A: name the quadrant of P and Q. Part B: explain why R is not in a quadrant.

  43. A club needs at least 4 members. Select all true statements for its membership count n. A. n ≥ 4 B. n > 4 C. 4 is an allowed membership count. D. 3 is an allowed membership count.

  44. A prism measures 13/2 m by 5/2 m by 1 m. Part A: enter its volume. Part B: how many cubes with edge 1/2 m fill it? Explain why the cube count differs from the cubic-meter volume.

  45. A quantity changes from a reference whole of 100 to a compared amount of 20. Part A: the compared amount is what percent of the reference whole? Part B: explain which number belongs in the denominator.

  46. Plot A(-2, -3), B(3, -3), and C(-2, 3). Part A: enter AB. Part B: enter AC and explain how zero helps find each distance.

  47. A machine uses 4 liters per hour. Part A: complete the (hours, liters) table: (0, __), (1, __), (2, __). Part B: choose the independent variable and write an equation for L in terms of h. Plot your pairs.

  48. Six students recorded reading times (minutes): 2, 8, 8, 14, 14, 28. Part A: describe center and spread using appropriate measures. Part B: describe shape, count, units, and any striking value; justify the chosen measures.

Answer key
  1. 47.7

    Align decimal points; 39.5 + 8.2 = 47.7.

  2. 4 × 4 × 4 = 64

    The exponent counts 3 factors of 4; multiply all factors.

  3. 1:1

    Keep red first; divide 5:5 by 5.

  4. -20 m; 0 means at sea level

    Sea level is the reference. Below is negative and above is positive.

  5. Terms 2x, 7y, 4; coefficients 2, 7; constant 4

    Terms are separated by addition; coefficients multiply variables.

  6. B, D - How does mass vary among the dogs?; What is the mass in kilograms of each of the dogs?

    Different dogs can have different mass. The questions anticipate variation across a collection. The conversion and arithmetic questions have fixed answers.

  7. 60

    30 ÷ 5: write 6, subtract 30, remainder 0; bring down the next digit: 0 ÷ 5: write 0, subtract 0, remainder 0. Check: 5 × 60 = 300.

  8. x = 4

    Subtract 2 on both sides. Then x = 4; substitution gives 6 on each side.

  9. B - 97/100

    Align decimal points; 2.6 - 1.63 = 0.97.

  10. C, D - (6, 4); (9, 6)

    Equivalent ratios multiply both quantities by the same factor. A uses 2; B uses 3. C and D change only one scaled quantity.

  11. 17

    Parentheses: 5 - 3 = 2. Exponent: 3² = 9. Multiply: 4 × 2 = 8. Add: 9 + 8 = 17. The product must be completed before the final sum.

  12. C - 336/125

    Compute the whole-number product and restore decimal places: 1.92 × 1.4 = 2.688.

  13. B, C - 7 × 5; (14 × 5) ÷ 2

    Triangle area is half the matching parallelogram: (14 × 5)/2 = 35 cm². Multiplying half the base by the height gives the same result.

  14. 5x + 2y - 2

    Marker cost is 5x; folder cost is 2y. Add these subtotals, then subtract the one fixed coupon of 2. An expression does not need an equals sign.

  15. D - 11/2

    Multiply both numbers by 10: 137.5 ÷ 25 = 5.5. Check: 2.5 × 5.5 = 13.75.

  16. Aligned pairs (0,0), (3,3), (6,6), (21,21); 21 m²

    Each aligned jump adds 3 liters and 3 m². Both quantities scale by 7; the number lines measure different units.

  17. 39/2

    Substitute: 7 × (3/2) + 3². The product is 21/2 and the square is 9. Add to get 39/2.

  18. B - 2

    Each small part is 1/4; 2 shaded parts divided into groups of 1 gives 2/1 = 2 groups.

  19. C, D - 3 notebooks cost $15.; Each notebook costs $5.

    Cost per notebook = 25 ÷ 5 = 5. Multiply $5 by the chosen notebook count.

  20. B, C - 2x + 8; 8 + 2x

    Distribute 2 to both terms to get 2x + 8. Reversing the addends gives the same value. The other expressions fail, for example at x = 0.

  21. D - 35/12

    7/4 ÷ 3/5 = 7/4 × 5/3 = 35/12.

  22. Removed 12 m²; remaining 88 m²

    Outer area = 100. Triangle = 4 × 6/2 = 12. Subtract the removed region exactly once.

  23. C, D - (5 + 6)x + 5; 11x + 5

    Combine coefficients of like x terms: 5 + 6 = 11. The constant 5 stays separate.

  24. A - 2

    5 ÷ 5/2 = 5 × 2/5 = 2.

  25. A: $4 each; B: $5 each; A is the better buy.

    Divide each cost by its own notebook count: 24/6 = 4; 35/7 = 5. Compare the same one-notebook unit.

  26. B, D - 6x + 24; 24 + 6x

    Distribute 6 to both terms to get 6x + 24. Reversing the addends gives the same value. The other expressions fail, for example at x = 0.

  27. A, D - 6 is the largest number of identical no-leftover kits from 12 pencils and 18 erasers.; 6 divides both 12 and 18.

    GCF(12, 18) = 6. Divide each count by 6: 2 pencils and 3 erasers per kit. A larger shared divisor is impossible.

  28. (5, 8, 19/2, 11, 14); IQR 3 min

    Minimum and maximum anchor whiskers. Box ends are 8, 11; median line is 19/2. IQR = 11 - 8.

  29. A, B - 18/4; 9/2

    Use the inverse operation on both sides. The solution is 9/2; the doubled numerator and denominator name the same value. Substitution verifies the solution.

  30. B, D - In 2 hours it travels 40 km.; The speed is 20 km per hour.

    Divide distance by time: 40 ÷ 2 = 20 km/h. Multiply this unit rate by 2 for the later distance.

  31. B, C - -9 < -7; -7 lies to the right of -9 on a number line.

    Among negatives, the number closer to zero is greater. Thus -7 is warmer and is farther right.

  32. A, D - 14/4; 7/2

    Use the inverse operation on both sides. The solution is 7/2; the doubled numerator and denominator name the same value. Substitution verifies the solution.

  33. A, C - 42 inches; 7/2 × 12 inches

    There are 12 smaller units in each larger one. Multiply 7/2 by 12, giving 42 inches. The physical length does not change.

  34. A, D - -9 is farther from zero than 7.; |-9| = 9

    Absolute value measures distance: 9 versus 7. A larger distance does not make the negative number greater.

  35. A, C - 26/4; 13/2

    Use the inverse operation on both sides. The solution is 13/2; the doubled numerator and denominator name the same value. Substitution verifies the solution.

  36. 12 participants; n = (15/100) × 80; less than the whole because the percent is below 100

    Rename 15% as 3/20. Multiply by 80 to obtain 12. Compare the rate with 1.

  37. 72 cm²; open box 60 cm²

    Closed area = 2(12+18+6) = 72. Subtract exactly one top face, 12, for the open box.

  38. 16 observations; highest intervals 10–14

    Use four equal-width touching bars with heights 3, 4, 5, 4. Frequencies sum to 16. Include every interval with frequency 5.

  39. 1 bottles; 2/3 L left

    2 ÷ 4/3 = 2 × 3/4 = 3/2. Only whole bottles count; subtract the liquid used.

  40. A, D - 5; 4

    Subtract 2 conceptually: x must exceed 3. Equality at 3 fails because the inequality is strict.

  41. A, C - (125/100)w = 75; w = 75 ÷ (125/100)

    The part is percent rate times whole. Divide 75 by 5/4; w = 60. Check by multiplication.

  42. P: II; Q: IV; R is on the y-axis.

    P has signs (-,+), so II. Q has (+,-), so IV. R has x = 0 and is on an axis.

  43. A, C - n ≥ 4; 4 is an allowed membership count.

    At least includes the boundary, so n ≥ 4. On a real number line use a closed point and shade right; actual member counts are whole numbers.

  44. 65/4 m³; 130 cubes

    Multiply edges: 13/2 × 5/2 × 1 = 65/4. The edge counts are 13, 5, 2, giving 130 cubes. Each cube is 1/8 m³; 130/8 = 65/4.

  45. 20%; denominator 100

    Percent = (20 ÷ 100) × 100 = 20%. The denominator is the reference whole, not automatically the larger number.

  46. AB = 5 units; AC = 6 units

    AB is horizontal: 2 + 3 = 5. AC is vertical: 3 + 3 = 6. Each segment crosses an axis.

  47. (0,0), (1,4), (2,8); independent h; L = 4h

    Hours are the input. Multiply each hour value by 4 to obtain liters. Graph hours horizontally and liters vertically.

  48. 6 observations; minutes; median 11, IQR 6; mean 37/3, MAD 19/3. A high value makes median/IQR a useful choice.

    Order the observations. Middle positions give median 11 and half medians give IQR 6. Mean is 74/6; averaging distances gives MAD 19/3. Tie the chosen summary to the displayed shape and context.
